## Complete GitHub Actions CI Pipeline Map for ars-ui

### Overview
You have **two workflow files** totaling 674 lines of YAML:
- **ci.yml**: Main CI pipeline (395 lines, 18 jobs)
- **nightly.yml**: Extended/nightly testing (279 lines, 4 job templates with matrix parallelization)

---

### 1. WORKFLOW FILES & TRIGGERS

#### **ci.yml** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`)
**Triggers:**
- `pull_request` (any PR)
- `push` to `main` branch
- `workflow_dispatch` (manual trigger)

**Concurrency & Cancellation:**
```yaml
concurrency:
  group: ci-${{ github.ref }}
  cancel-in-progress: true
```
Lines 10-12: Cancels in-progress CI runs when a new push/PR arrives on the same ref.

---

#### **nightly.yml** (`.github/workflows/nightly.yml`)
**Triggers:**
- `schedule`: `0 3 * * *` (03:00 UTC daily)
- `workflow_dispatch` (manual trigger)

**Concurrency & Cancellation:**
```yaml
concurrency:
  group: nightly-${{ github.ref }}
  cancel-in-progress: true
```
Lines 8-10: Same cancel-in-progress pattern as ci.yml

---

### 4. JOB DEPENDENCY GRAPH & PARALLELIZATION

```
fmt ──────────┬──────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┐
              ↓          ↓                                       │
           clippy     check                                    mutual-exclusion
              │          │                                       │
              └─→ unit ←──┘                                     (separate)
                   │
        ┌──────────┼──────────────────┬────────────────┐
        ↓          ↓                  ↓                ↓
   snapshot-    i18n-browser    integration    error-variant-coverage
   change       dom-browser     adapter
   check        release         adapter-parity
              coverage (90 min)     + 5× feature-flags (shared cache)
              
   feature-flag-check (aggregates all feature-flags-*)
```

**Parallelization details:**
- **Stage 1 (parallel):** `fmt` only
- **Stage 2 (parallel):** `clippy` + `check` (both need `fmt`)
- **Stage 3 (sequential gate):** `unit` (needs both `clippy` + `check`)
- **Stage 4 (highly parallel):** ~14 jobs all need `unit`:
  - snapshot-change-check (conditional, PR-only)
  - i18n-browser, dom-browser, release, mutual-exclusion
  - integration, adapter, adapter-parity, error-variant-coverage
  - coverage (90-min timeout, blocks codecov upload)
  - 5× feature-flags-* (shared cache, parallel)
- **Stage 5 (final):** `feature-flag-check` (aggregation, no cargo)

**Total serial bottlenecks:** 3 stages (fmt → clippy/check → unit)

---

### 5. REDUNDANCY & OPPORTUNITIES

#### Redundancy Found:

**A. `check` and `clippy` are sequential but could be parallel (lines 37-61)**
```yaml
# clippy (line 39)
needs: [fmt]
# check (line 53)
needs: [fmt]
```
Both check and clippy only need `fmt`. They both:
- Run stable toolchain
- Use same cache (Swatinem/rust-cache@v2)
- Install desktop deps
- Take ~2-5 min each

**Optimization:** Remove the implicit serial dependency by running them in parallel after `fmt`. This would save ~3-5 minutes on the critical path if one is faster than the other.

---

**B. `unit` gates 11+ downstream jobs (lines 63-79)**
```yaml
needs: [check, clippy]
```
But `unit` itself is a gate that all these jobs wait for:
- i18n-browser, dom-browser, release, integration, adapter, adapter-parity
- error-variant-coverage, coverage, all 5 feature-flags-*

**Question:** Can any of these jobs be parallelized without waiting for `unit`? For example:
- Does `release` actually need the unit tests to pass? (line 149: `needs: [unit]`)
- Does `coverage` compilation depend on unit tests, or just on source code? (line 220: `needs: [unit]`)

If these can run in parallel with `unit` instead of after, you save ~10-15 minutes.

---

**C. `coverage` job (90-min timeout) is the slowest single job (lines 218-288)**
```yaml
timeout-minutes: 90
```
This job:
- Compiles for x86_64 + wasm32 with LLVM instrumentation
- Installs LLVM 22 from source (not cached)
- Runs `cargo xtask ci coverage` (full test coverage)
- Uploads to Codecov

**Optimization opportunities:**
1. Cache LLVM 22 installation step (lines 248-253)
2. Parallelize coverage compilation: separate native + wasm coverage into 2 jobs? (requires splitting coverage.rs script)
3. Run coverage off-path for non-main branches? (only on `main` + scheduled nightly)

---

**D. Desktop dependencies installed 5 times (lines 47, 59, 72, 156, 192, 365)**
```yaml
- uses: ./.github/actions/install-dioxus-desktop-deps
```
Jobs: clippy, check, unit, release, adapter, feature-flags-dioxus

Each time runs:
```bash
sudo apt-get update
sudo apt-get install -y libgtk-3-dev libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libayatana-appindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev libxdo-dev
```

**Optimization:** These are cached at the OS level, but `apt-get update` still runs every time. Consider:
1. Cache the apt state across workflow runs (e.g., using a Docker image with pre-installed deps)
2. Add a `--no-update` flag to skip update on cache hit

---

**E. `feature-flags-*` jobs all use `shared-key: feature-flags` (lines 299, 314-315, 330-331, 345, 361)**
```yaml
uses: Swatinem/rust-cache@v2
with:
  shared-key: feature-flags
```
This is **good** — they share the cargo registry/git cache, but each job still:
- Checks out code (5 redundant checkouts)
- Sets up toolchain (5× dtolnay actions)
- Installs nextest (5×)

**Question:** Could these 5 jobs be merged into 1 with a matrix strategy? If so:
- 1 checkout instead of 5
- 1 toolchain setup instead of 5
- 1 nextest install instead of 5
- Shared cache key (already have this)

**Trade-off:** Slightly longer job name in logs, but much faster overall if they run serially within the same job.

---

### 6. CACHING STRATEGY

**Primary cache:** `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2` (used in 15/18 jobs)

**Cache keys:**
- Default key (most jobs): Auto-generated from `Cargo.lock` + runner OS
- Shared key (feature-flags jobs): `shared-key: feature-flags` (lines 299, 315, 331, 346, 362)

**Cache paths (default Swatinem behavior):**
- `~/.cargo/registry`
- `~/.cargo/git`
- `target/`

**Cache performance:**
- ✅ Good: Swatinem is well-maintained and handles incremental cargo builds
- ⚠️ Watch: No `sccache` used (incremental compiler cache across runs)
- ⚠️ Watch: `coverage` job purges `/opt/` dirs (lines 238-246), potentially invalidating some caches
- ✅ Good: `feature-flags-*` jobs share cache, avoiding 5× registry rebuilds

**Potential issues:**
1. No sccache for distributed compilation caching (if you scale to multiple runners)
2. Coverage job's aggressive disk cleanup might invalidate intermediate artifacts

---

### 7. TOOLCHAIN PINNING

**File:** `rust-toolchain.toml` (lines 1-3)
```toml
[toolchain]
channel = "stable"
components = ["rustfmt", "clippy"]
```

**Summary:**
- **Channel:** `stable` (auto-updates to latest stable weekly)
- **Components:** rustfmt, clippy
- **No MSRV pinning** (e.g., `1.70.0`) — always latest stable

**In ci.yml:**
- `fmt` job: Overrides with `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@nightly` (line 20) + stable (line 23)
- `coverage` job: Uses both `stable` (line 225) + `nightly` (line 228)
- All other jobs: Use `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable` (respects rust-toolchain.toml)

**Implication:**
- CI is sensitive to Rust compiler changes (weekly updates)
- Good for catching breakage early, but adds noise if unstable features are used

---

### 8. NIGHTLY.yml BREAKDOWN

**Total jobs: 4 job templates** with matrix parallelization

---

#### Job: `extended-proptest` (lines 13-34)
**Trigger:** Schedule (daily) + manual
**Steps:**
1. `checkout@v6`
2. `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable`
3. `actions/cache@v5` (NOT Swatinem; uses manual key):
   ```yaml
   key: ${{ runner.os }}-cargo-nightly-proptest-${{ hashFiles('**/Cargo.lock') }}
   ```
4. **Command:** `cargo test -p ars-core -p ars-components --features ars-components/i18n -- --ignored proptest` (line 34)

**Env:** `PROPTEST_CASES: 10000` (line 17)

**Cache:** `actions/cache@v5` with **custom key** (different from Swatinem)

---

#### Job: `a11y-audit` (lines 36-60)
**Steps:**
1. `checkout@v6`
2. `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable` with wasm32-unknown-unknown
3. `actions/cache@v5` with custom key including `a11y`:
   ```yaml
   key: ${{ runner.os }}-cargo-nightly-a11y-${{ hashFiles('**/Cargo.lock') }}
   ```
   Also caches `~/.cache/wasm-pack` (line 49)
4. `taiki-e/install-action@v2` → wasm-pack@0.14.0
5. **Commands:**
   - `wasm-pack test --headless --chrome crates/ars-leptos --test axe`
   - `wasm-pack test --headless --chrome crates/ars-dioxus --test axe`

**Cache:** Includes wasm-pack cache

---

#### Job: `mutation-testing` (lines 62-240)
**Matrix:** 18 matrix entries (12 shards of ars-components, 4 shards of ars-collections, 2 shards of ars-core, 3 single-job runs)

**Matrix variables:**
```yaml
matrix:
  include:
    - name: components-shard-1
      package: ars-components
      shard: "0/12"
      features: "ars-components/i18n"
    ... (17 more entries)
```

**Steps (for each matrix entry):**
1. `checkout@v6`
2. `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable`
3. `actions/cache@v5` with key **keyed by package, not shard** (line 204):
   ```yaml
   key: ${{ runner.os }}-cargo-nightly-mutants-${{ matrix.package }}-${{ hashFiles('**/Cargo.lock') }}
   ```
   **All shards of the same package share the same cache.**
4. `taiki-e/install-action@v2` → cargo-mutants@27.0.0 (pinned version, line 212)
5. **Build & run command (lines 213-231):**
   ```bash
   args=(-p "$PACKAGE" --output mutants.out)
   if [ -n "$FEATURES" ]; then
     args+=(--features "$FEATURES")
   fi
   if [ -n "$SHARD" ]; then
     cargo mutants "${args[@]}" --shard "$SHARD"
   else
     cargo mutants "${args[@]}"
   fi
   ```
6. **Upload artifacts (lines 234-240):** Always upload `mutants.out/`, retain for 14 days

**Parallelization:**
- 18 parallel runners (one per matrix entry)
- Each shard of ars-components runs in parallel, sharing registry cache
- ~30 min per shard (comments line 87)
- Slowest shard targets ~50 min for future scaling (line 90)

**Failure strategy:** `fail-fast: false` (line 68) — all shards run even if one fails

---

#### Job: `cross-compile` (lines 242-279)
**Matrix:** 3 entries (wasm32 + web-intl leptos, wasm32 + web-intl dioxus, x86_64 + icu4x)

**Matrix:**
```yaml
matrix:
  include:
    - name: wasm32 + web-intl (leptos)
      target: wasm32-unknown-unknown
      features: "-p ars-leptos --no-default-features --features ars-leptos/csr,ars-i18n/web-intl"
    - name: wasm32 + web-intl (dioxus)
      target: wasm32-unknown-unknown
      features: "-p ars-dioxus --no-default-features --features ars-dioxus/web,ars-i18n/web-intl"
    - name: native + icu4x
      target: x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu
      features: "-p ars-i18n --no-default-features --features std,icu4x"
```

**Steps (for each):**
1. `checkout@v6`
2. `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable` with target
3. `actions/cache@v5` with custom key:
   ```yaml
   key: ${{ runner.os }}-cargo-nightly-cross-${{ matrix.target }}-${{ hashFiles('**/Cargo.lock') }}
   ```
4. **Command:** `cargo check --target ${{ matrix.target }} ${{ matrix.features }}`

**Purpose:** Verify feature combinations don't break compilation on different targets

### 9. SUMMARY: TIMEOUTS & CONCURRENCY

| Setting | Value | Location |
|---------|-------|----------|
| **CI concurrency group** | `ci-${{ github.ref }}` | ci.yml:11 |
| **CI cancel-in-progress** | `true` | ci.yml:12 |
| **Nightly concurrency group** | `nightly-${{ github.ref }}` | nightly.yml:9 |
| **Nightly cancel-in-progress** | `true` | nightly.yml:10 |
| **Coverage timeout** | 90 minutes | ci.yml:222 |
| **Mutation testing failures** | `fail-fast: false` | nightly.yml:68 |
| **Cross-compile failures** | `fail-fast: false` | nightly.yml:246 |

### 10. KEY OPTIMIZATIONS FOR SPEED

**High-impact (5-15 min savings each):**
1. **Parallelize `check` + `clippy`** — Remove serial dependency, they both follow `fmt`
2. **Parallelize downstream test jobs** — Some may not strictly need `unit` to pass first
3. **Cache LLVM 22** in coverage job (currently rebuilt every run)
4. **Merge feature-flags jobs into 1 with matrix** — 5 checkouts → 1

**Medium-impact (2-5 min savings each):**
5. **Cache apt-get state** for desktop dependencies
6. **Run coverage off-path** — Only on main/schedule, not every PR
7. **Profile `cargo xtask` scripts** — These abstract commands may have redundancy

**Low-impact:**
8. Use sccache for distributed compilation (requires setup)
9. Split coverage into native + wasm parallel jobs

---

This is your complete CI map. The pipeline is well-structured with good concurrency design, but there are clear optimization opportunities in parallelization and caching.

### 6. SUBPROCESS INVOCATION PATTERNS

xtask uses **two primary patterns** to shell out to cargo and external tools:

#### **Pattern A: Direct `cargo` via `process::Command` (Module-Level)**

**In `ci/mod.rs`** (lines 858-877):
```rust
pub(crate) fn cargo(step: Step, args: &[&str]) -> Result<(), Error> {
    let display_cmd = format!("cargo {}", args.join(" "));
    eprintln!("  > {display_cmd}");
    
    let status = process::Command::new("cargo")
        .args(args)
        .status()
        .map_err(Error::Io)?;
    
    if status.success() {
        Ok(())
    } else {
        Err(Error::StepFailed {
            step,
            command: display_cmd,
            code: status.code(),
        })
    }
}

// Wrapper with --message-format injection (line 829):
fn cargo_with_format(step: Step, args: &[&str], message_format: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), Error>
```

**Usage**: `cargo(Step::Fmt, &["+nightly", "fmt", "--all", "--check"])` (line 455)

#### **Pattern B: Streaming Command with Capture** (test.rs)

**In `test.rs`** (lines 621-679):
```rust
fn run_nextest_command(label: &str, args: &[&str]) -> Result<CommandResult, Error> {
    eprintln!("  [nextest] {label}");
    
    let mut command = process::Command::new("cargo");
    command
        .args(["nextest", "run", "--no-fail-fast"])
        .args(args);
    
    configure_color_output(&mut command);
    
    let output = run_streaming_process(command)?;  // Captures stdout/stderr
    
    // Parse nextest summary from output via regex...
    let summary = parse_nextest_summary(&combined);
    
    Ok(CommandResult {
        command: display,
        code: output.code,
        success: output.success,
        summary,
        output: combined,
    })
}

// Helper: spawn, inherit stdio, capture into threads (lines 697-735)
fn run_streaming_process(mut command: process::Command) -> Result<CommandOutput, Error> {
    command.stdout(Stdio::piped()).stderr(Stdio::piped());
    
    let mut child = command.spawn().map_err(Error::Io)?;
    
    // Two threads tee stdout/stderr to both screen and capture buffer...
    let stdout_thread = thread::spawn(move || tee_reader(stdout, io::stdout()));
    let stderr_thread = thread::spawn(move || tee_reader(stderr, io::stderr()));
    
    // Wait for child & threads, return combined output...
}
```

#### **Pattern C: External Tool Invocation** (Any module)

**Example in `mutants.rs`** (lines 110-157):
```rust
pub fn run(options: &Options) -> Result<(), Error> {
    preflight_cargo_mutants()?;  // Verify tool exists (lines 159-174)
    
    let mut command = process::Command::new("cargo");
    command
        .arg("mutants")
        .arg("-p")
        .arg(&options.package)
        .arg("--output")
        .arg(&options.output);
    
    // ... add optional flags ...
    
    command.stdin(Stdio::inherit());
    command.stdout(Stdio::inherit());
    command.stderr(Stdio::inherit());
    
    let status = command.status()?;
    
    if status.success() {
        Ok(())
    } else {
        Err(Error::CommandFailed {
            code: status.code(),
        })
    }
}

fn preflight_cargo_mutants() -> Result<(), Error> {
    let output = process::Command::new("cargo")
        .args(["mutants", "--version"])
        .output()?;
    
    if output.status.success() {
        return Ok(());
    }
    
    Err(Error::MissingTool {
        tool: "cargo-mutants".into(),
        install_hint: format!(
            "cargo install cargo-mutants --locked --version {CARGO_MUTANTS_VERSION}"
        ),
    })
}
```

#### **Pattern D: Shell Commands with stdin/stdout Capture**

**In `ci/mod.rs`** (lines 1071-1099):
```rust
fn capture_command_stdout(program: &str, args: &[&str], input: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<u8>, Error> {
    let display_cmd = format!("{program} {}", args.join(" "));
    
    let mut child = process::Command::new(program)
        .args(args)
        .stdin(Stdio::piped())
        .stdout(Stdio::piped())
        .spawn()
        .map_err(Error::Io)?;
    
    child
        .stdin
        .take()
        .expect("formatter stdin should be piped")
        .write_all(input)
        .map_err(Error::Io)?;
    
    let output = child.wait_with_output().map_err(Error::Io)?;
    
    if output.status.success() {
        Ok(output.stdout)
    } else {
        Err(Error::StepFailed { ... })
    }
}
```

**Used by**:
- `leptosfmt` for Leptos view! formatting (line 328)
- `dx fmt` for Dioxus rsx! formatting (line 336)
- `rustfmt` for final Rust formatting (line 332)

### 7. HOW TO WIRE IN A NEW TOOL (e.g., `cargo-crap`)

To add a new external tool like `cargo-crap`, follow this pattern:

**Step 1: Define Preflight Check** (in relevant module)
```rust
fn preflight_cargo_crap() -> Result<(), Error> {
    let output = process::Command::new("cargo")
        .args(["crap", "--version"])
        .output()
        .map_err(Error::Io)?;
    
    if !output.success() {
        return Err(Error::MissingTool {
            tool: "cargo-crap".into(),
            install_hint: "cargo install cargo-crap --locked".into(),
        });
    }
    
    Ok(())
}
```

**Step 2: Create Step Invocation Function**
```rust
fn run_crap() -> Result<(), Error> {
    preflight_cargo_crap()?;
    
    cargo(Step::SomeNewStep, &["crap", "--some-flag"])
}
```

Or for external tools (non-cargo):
```rust
fn run_crap() -> Result<(), Error> {
    let mut command = process::Command::new("crap");
    command.args(["--some-flag", "--another-flag"]);
    
    let status = command.status().map_err(Error::Io)?;
    
    if status.success() {
        Ok(())
    } else {
        Err(Error::StepFailed {
            step: Step::SomeNewStep,
            command: "crap --some-flag --another-flag".into(),
            code: status.code(),
        })
    }
}
```

**Step 3: Add to Step Enum** (`ci/mod.rs`, lines 30-98)
```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, clap::ValueEnum)]
pub enum Step {
    // ... existing steps ...
    /// Run cargo-crap on the workspace.
    Crap,
}
```

**Step 4: Add Step Name** (line 1207-1230)
```rust
const fn step_name(step: Step) -> &'static str {
    match step {
        // ...
        Step::Crap => "crap",
    }
}
```

**Step 5: Add to Dispatch** (`run_step()`, line 393-440)
```rust
fn run_step(step: Step, message_format: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), Error> {
    match step {
        // ...
        Step::Crap => run_crap(),
    }
}
```

**Step 6: Optionally Add to Pipeline**
```rust
const PIPELINE_ORDER: &[Step] = &[
    // ... existing steps ...
    Step::Crap,  // or wherever in the order
    // ... more steps ...
];
```

# cargo-crap: A Rust Complexity and Coverage Analysis Tool

## Problem It Solves

`cargo-crap` addresses a critical gap in Rust code safety: determining whether code is safe to modify. While Rust prevents many bugs through memory and thread safety, it cannot answer whether a function is risky to change. The tool identifies functions that combine high cyclomatic complexity with poor test coverage—a dangerous combination, especially in AI-assisted development where code changes happen rapidly.

## What It Does

The tool calculates the CRAP (Change Risk Anti-Patterns) metric by combining two signals:
- **Cyclomatic complexity**: counts independent execution paths through a function
- **Test coverage**: indicates which code paths were exercised by tests

As the article states, the formula produces a single score where "complex and untested code receives a much higher score," making risky functions immediately visible during code review.

## Installation

Two methods are available:

**Via pre-built binary (requires cargo-binstall):**
```
cargo binstall cargo-crap
```

**From source:**
```
cargo install cargo-crap
```

The crate name on crates.io is `cargo-crap`.

## Command-Line Usage

The basic two-command workflow:

```
cargo llvm-cov --lcov --output-path lcov.info
cargo crap --lcov lcov.info
```

**Key flags mentioned:**
- `--lcov <path>`: Path to LCOV coverage report
- `--fail-above`: Fail build when threshold exceeded
- `--threshold <number>`: CRAP score threshold (default: 30)
- `--fail-regression`: Fail only on regressions from baseline
- `--baseline <path>`: Baseline JSON for regression detection
- `--format <type>`: Output format options include:
  - `github` (GitHub Actions annotations)
  - `pr-comment` (pull-request comment format)
  - `json` (machine-readable output)
- `--output <path>`: Write output to file

## Output

The tool produces a ranked table showing:
- CRAP score
- Cyclomatic complexity (CC)
- Coverage percentage (visual bar)
- Function name
- Source location

Functions are flagged with symbols (✗ for exceeding threshold, ▲ for moderate risk, ✓ for safe).

## Exit Codes

The article does not explicitly detail exit codes, only that the tool can fail a build when thresholds are crossed.

## Configuration

No configuration file format is mentioned in the article.

## CI Integration Guidance

For new projects, use absolute thresholds:
```yaml
cargo crap --lcov lcov.info --fail-above --threshold 30
```

For mature codebases, the article recommends baseline mode to prevent regressions rather than blocking all high-complexity code. A provided GitHub Actions example shows a minimal CI setup that runs coverage collection followed by the CRAP analysis.
```

```
Compute the CRAP (Change Risk Anti-Patterns) metric for Rust projects.

Usage: cargo-crap [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --lcov <FILE>
          Path to an LCOV coverage file (e.g. from `cargo llvm-cov --lcov --output-path lcov.info`).
          
          If omitted, every function is scored as if it had 0% coverage — useful for a first look at complexity distribution but not a real CRAP run.

      --path <DIR>
          Root directory to analyze. Defaults to the current directory
          
          [default: .]

      --workspace
          Analyze all workspace members discovered via `cargo metadata`.
          
          When set, `--path` is ignored and every member crate's root is walked. Useful for monorepos: one command, one LCOV file, full workspace picture.

      --exclude <GLOB>
          Glob patterns for files to skip (relative to `--path`). Use `**` to cross directory boundaries. May be repeated.
          
          Examples: `--exclude 'tests/**'`  `--exclude 'src/generated/**'`

      --threshold <THRESHOLD>
          CRAP score above which a function is considered "crappy". Falls back to `.cargo-crap.toml` → built-in default (30)

      --min <SCORE>
          Only print functions with a CRAP score above this cutoff

      --top <N>
          Limit the report to the top N crappiest functions

      --missing <MISSING>
          How to handle functions with complexity data but no coverage data. Falls back to `.cargo-crap.toml` → built-in default (pessimistic)
          
          [possible values: pessimistic, optimistic, skip]

      --format <FORMAT>
          Output format

          Possible values:
          - human
          - json
          - github:     GitHub Actions workflow commands — one `::warning` per crappy function
          - markdown:   GitHub-Flavored Markdown table — exhaustive, no row caps. Use this for archived artifacts and docs pages
          - pr-comment: Opinionated PR-comment markdown — hides unchanged rows, caps each section, collapses non-critical info into `<details>` blocks. Designed for the GitHub PR comment use case where readability beats completeness
          - sarif:      SARIF 2.1.0 JSON for upload to GitHub Code Scanning, VS Code, and other static-analysis tools. Each crappy function becomes one warning-level result. Incompatible with `--baseline`
          
          [default: human]

      --summary
          Print only aggregate statistics (total analyzed, crappy count, worst offender) — no per-function table. Compatible with all `--format` values except `json` and `github` (which are unaffected)

      --fail-above
          Exit with a non-zero status if any function's CRAP score exceeds `--threshold`. Useful in CI

      --allow <GLOB>
          Suppress functions matching these glob patterns. May be repeated.
          
          An entry containing `/` or `**` is treated as a path glob and matches the file in which the function is defined; otherwise it matches the function name. Path globs analyze the file but hide its functions from the report — distinct from `--exclude`, which skips files at walk time.
          
          Examples: `--allow 'Foo::*'`  `--allow 'generated_*'` `--allow 'src/generated/**'`  `--allow 'tests/**'`

      --baseline <FILE>
          JSON baseline from a previous `--format json` run. When provided the report shows per-function deltas (regressions, improvements, new)

      --fail-regression
          Exit non-zero if any function's CRAP score increased since `--baseline`. Requires `--baseline`

      --output <FILE>
          Write output to FILE instead of stdout. Useful for saving a JSON baseline: `--format json --output baseline.json`

      --jobs <N>
          Maximum number of threads used for parallel source-file analysis. Defaults to rayon's host-sized pool. Useful in memory-constrained CI/Docker environments. Falls back to `.cargo-crap.toml` → host default

      --epsilon <VALUE>
          Tolerance used by the regression detector. CRAP-score deltas with absolute value at or below this count as `Unchanged` rather than `Regressed` / `Improved`. Falls back to `.cargo-crap.toml` → built-in default (0.01)

      --repo-url <URL>
          Base URL of the source-hosting repo (e.g. `https://github.com/owner/repo`). Combined with `--commit-ref`, makes Function and Location cells in `pr-comment` / `markdown` output clickable links to GitHub source. Defaults from `GITHUB_SERVER_URL` + `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` when those env vars are set (e.g. inside GitHub Actions)

      --commit-ref <REF>
          Commit SHA or branch name to deep-link into. Defaults from `GITHUB_SHA` when set. Has no effect unless `--repo-url` is also set

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

  -V, --version
          Print version
===CRAP SUBCMD===

          Print only aggregate statistics (total analyzed, crappy count, worst offender) — no per-function table. Compatible with all `--format` values except `json` and `github` (which are unaffected)

          Exit with a non-zero status if any function's CRAP score exceeds `--threshold`. Useful in CI

Path globs analyze the file but hide its functions from the report — distinct from `--exclude`, which skips files at walk time.
          
          Examples: `--allow 'Foo::*'`  `--allow 'generated_*'` `--allow 'src/generated/**'`  `--allow 'tests/**'`

      --baseline <FILE>
          JSON baseline from a previous `--format json` run. When provided the report shows per-function deltas (regressions, improvements, new)

          Exit non-zero if any function's CRAP score increased since `--baseline`. Requires `--baseline`

          Write output to FILE instead of stdout. Useful for saving a JSON baseline: `--format json --output baseline.json`

      --jobs <N>
          Maximum number of threads used for parallel source-file analysis. Defaults to rayon's host-sized pool. Useful in memory-constrained CI/Docker environments. Falls back to `.cargo-crap.toml` → host default

      --epsilon <VALUE>
          Tolerance used by the regression detector. CRAP-score deltas with absolute value at or below this count as `Unchanged` rather than `Regressed` / `Improved`. Falls back to `.cargo-crap.toml` → built-in default (0.01)

      --repo-url <URL>
          Base URL of the source-hosting repo (e.g. `https://github.com/owner/repo`). Combined with `--commit-ref`, makes Function and Location cells in `pr-comment` / `markdown` output clickable links to GitHub source. Defaults from `GITHUB_SERVER_URL` + `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` when those env vars are set (e.g. inside GitHub Actions)

      --commit-ref <REF>
          Commit SHA or branch name to deep-link into. Defaults from `GITHUB_SHA` when set. Has no effect unless `--repo-url` is also set

  -h, --help

25	/// CI pipeline steps, matching the GitHub Actions job names.
26	///
27	/// Steps are listed in pipeline dependency order for sequential local
28	/// execution. Use [`run`] to execute one or more steps with fail-fast
29	/// semantics.
30	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, clap::ValueEnum)]
31	#[value(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
32	pub enum Step {
33	    /// `leptosfmt --check ...`, `dx fmt --check ...`, and
34	    /// `cargo +nightly fmt --all --check`
35	    Fmt,
36	
37	    /// `cargo check --workspace --all-features`
38	    Check,
39

41	    Clippy,
42	
43	    /// Unit tests for core crates.
44	    Unit,
45

47	    I18nBrowser,
48	
49	    /// Browser-executed wasm tests for ars-dom's web feature.
50	    DomBrowser,
51	
52	    /// Release-profile compile and test smoke checks.
53	    Release,
54	
55	    /// Integration tests.
56	    Integration,
57	
58	    /// Adapter harness tests (Leptos + Dioxus).
59	    Adapter,
60	
61	    /// Compare per-component test counts between adapters.
62	    AdapterParity,
63	
64	    /// Generate coverage and check per-crate thresholds.
65	    Coverage,
66	
67	    /// Enforce per-component snapshot-count policy.
68	    SnapshotCount,
69	
70	    /// Parse every fenced Rust code block in the spec corpus and report
71	    /// any `syn::parse_file` syntax errors. Catches structural Rust bugs
72	    /// in spec snippets before they ship.
73	    SpecCompileSnippets,
74	
75	    /// Verify every `ComponentError` variant appears in tests.
76	    ErrorVariantCoverage,
77	
78	    /// Meta-step: run all five feature-matrix groups.
79	    FeatureMatrix,
80	
81	    /// Feature flags — ars-core (15 combos).
82	    FeatureMatrixCore,
83

100	/// Pipeline profile selecting which step list to run when no positional steps
101	/// are passed.
102	///
103	/// - [`Profile::Full`] mirrors the GitHub Actions CI job exactly (20 gates,
104	///   ~25 minutes locally) and is the default for backward compatibility with
105	///   scripts that call `cargo xci` directly.
106	/// - [`Profile::Fast`] runs the curated [`FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`] subset
107	///   (~5 minutes) intended as the routine pre-push gate. It drops the coverage
108	///   recompile, the wasm browser tests, the release-profile checks, and the
109	///   five `feature-matrix-*` groups — all of which are CI-only concerns that
110	///   re-test what `Unit` / `Integration` / `Adapter` already covered.
111	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default, clap::ValueEnum)]
112	#[value(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
113	pub enum Profile {
114	    /// Full CI parity (default). Runs every step in [`PIPELINE_ORDER`].
115	    #[default]
116	    Full,
117	
118	    /// Fast pre-push gate. Runs every step in [`FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`].
119	    Fast,
120	}
121	
122	/// Default pipeline order when no steps are specified and [`Profile::Full`]
123	/// is selected.
124	///
125	/// `FeatureMatrix` is intentionally absent — the pipeline runs each sub-group
126	/// individually so progress is visible per group.
127	const PIPELINE_ORDER: &[Step] = &[
128	    Step::Fmt,
129	    Step::Check,
130	    Step::Clippy,
131	    Step::Unit,
132	    Step::I18nBrowser,
133	    Step::DomBrowser,
134	    Step::Release,
135	    Step::Integration,
136	    Step::Adapter,
137	    Step::AdapterParity,
138	    Step::Coverage,
139	    Step::SnapshotCount,
140	    Step::SpecCompileSnippets,
141	    Step::ErrorVariantCoverage,
142	    Step::FeatureMatrixCore,
143	    Step::FeatureMatrixI18n,
144	    Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems,
145	    Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos,
146	    Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus,
147	    Step::MutualExclusion,
148	];
149	
150	/// Curated pipeline order when [`Profile::Fast`] is selected and no positional
151	/// steps are passed.
152	///
153	/// Covers cross-crate / workspace-level regressions and the structural lints
154	/// that gate merge, while skipping the wasm-coverage recompile, the wasm
155	/// browser tests, the release-profile checks, and the five `feature-matrix-*`
156	/// groups — those run in CI and rarely change the outcome of a local pre-push
157	/// gate when `Unit`, `Integration`, `Adapter`, and `--all-features check`
158	/// have already passed.
159	///
160	/// Power users who touched wasm or feature-gated code can opt the skipped
161	/// steps back in positionally, e.g. `cargo xci --profile fast i18n-browser`;
162	/// positional steps still override the profile.
163	const FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER: &[Step] = &[
164	    Step::Fmt,
165	    Step::Check,
166	    Step::Clippy,
167	    Step::Unit,
168	    Step::Integration,
169	    Step::Adapter,
170	    Step::SpecCompileSnippets,
171	    Step::ErrorVariantCoverage,
172	    Step::MutualExclusion,
173	    Step::SnapshotCount,
174	];
175

189	    },
190	
191	    /// A required tool is not installed.

194	        tool: String,
195	
196	        /// How to install it.
197	        install_hint: String,
198	    },
199	
200	    /// IO error spawning a subprocess.

355	///   [`Profile::Full`], [`FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`] for [`Profile::Fast`]).
356	/// - `FeatureMatrix` → expands to the five individual groups.
357	/// - Everything else passes through as-is.
358	///
359	/// Positional steps always override the profile: passing `vec![Step::Fmt]`
360	/// with `Profile::Fast` yields `vec![Step::Fmt]`, not the fast subset.
361	fn resolve_steps(steps: Vec<Step>, profile: Profile) -> Vec<Step> {
362	    if steps.is_empty() {
363	        return match profile {
364	            Profile::Full => PIPELINE_ORDER.to_vec(),
365	            Profile::Fast => FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER.to_vec(),
366	        };
367	    }
368	
369	    let mut resolved = Vec::with_capacity(steps.len());
370	
371	    for step in steps {
372	        if step == Step::FeatureMatrix {
373	            resolved.extend_from_slice(&[
374	                Step::FeatureMatrixCore,
375	                Step::FeatureMatrixI18n,
376	                Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems,
377	                Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos,
378	                Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus,
379	            ]);
380	        } else {
381	            resolved.push(step);
382	        }
383	    }
384	
385	    resolved
386	}
387	
388	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
389	// Step dispatch
390	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
391	
392	/// Execute a single CI step.
393	fn run_step(step: Step, message_format: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), Error> {
394	    match step {
395	        Step::Fmt => run_fmt(),
396	
397	        Step::Check => run_check(message_format),
398	
399	        Step::Clippy => run_clippy(message_format),
400	
401	        Step::Unit => run_unit(),
402	
403	        Step::I18nBrowser => run_i18n_browser(),
404	
405	        Step::DomBrowser => run_dom_browser(),
406	
407	        Step::Release => run_release(),
408	
409	        Step::Integration => run_integration(),
410	
411	        Step::Adapter => run_adapter(),
412	
413	        Step::AdapterParity => run_adapter_parity(),
414	
415	        Step::Coverage => run_coverage(),
416	
417	        Step::SnapshotCount => run_snapshot_count(),
418	
419	        Step::SpecCompileSnippets => run_spec_compile_snippets(),
420	
421	        Step::ErrorVariantCoverage => run_error_variant_coverage(),
422	
423	        Step::FeatureMatrix => {
424	            unreachable!("FeatureMatrix is expanded by resolve_steps")
425	        }
426	
427	        Step::FeatureMatrixCore => feature_matrix::run_group(feature_matrix::Group::Core),
428	
429	        Step::FeatureMatrixI18n => feature_matrix::run_group(feature_matrix::Group::I18n),
430	
431	        Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems => {
432	            feature_matrix::run_group(feature_matrix::Group::Subsystems)
433	        }
434	
435	        Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos => feature_matrix::run_group(feature_matrix::Group::Leptos),
436	
437	        Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus => feature_matrix::run_group(feature_matrix::Group::Dioxus),
438	
439	        Step::MutualExclusion => run_mutual_exclusion(),
440	    }
441	}
442	
443	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
444	// Individual step implementations
445	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
446	
447	fn run_fmt() -> Result<(), Error> {
448	    preflight_nightly()?;
449	    preflight_leptosfmt()?;
450	    preflight_dioxus_cli()?;
451	
452	    leptosfmt_check()?;
453	    dioxusfmt(true)?;
454	
455	    cargo(Step::Fmt, &["+nightly", "fmt", "--all", "--check"])
456	}
457	
458	fn run_check(message_format: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), Error> {
459	    // Exclude ars-i18n: its `icu4x` and `web-intl` features are mutually
460	    // exclusive, so `--all-features` would trigger a compile_error!.
461	    // Instead, check ars-i18n twice — once per backend — to cover all features.
462	    cargo_with_format(
463	        Step::Check,
464	        &[
465	            "check",
466	            "--workspace",
467	            "--all-features",
468	            "--exclude",
469	            "ars-i18n",
470	        ],
471	        message_format,
472	    )?;
473	
474	    let [icu4x_features, web_intl_features] = i18n::i18n_feature_lists().map_err(Error::Io)?;
475	
476	    for features in [&icu4x_features, &web_intl_features] {
477	        cargo_with_format(
478	            Step::Check,
479	            &[
480	                "check",
481	                "-p",
482	                "ars-i18n",
483	                "--all-targets",
484	                "--no-default-features",
485	                "--features",
486	                features,
487	            ],
488	            message_format,
489	        )?;
490	    }
491	
492	    Ok(())
493	}
494	
495	fn run_clippy(message_format: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), Error> {
496	    clippy_workspace(message_format, true)
497	}
498	
499	/// Run workspace-wide clippy with ars-i18n backend splitting.
500	///
501	/// Used by both the CI `clippy` step (`deny_warnings = true`) and
502	/// `cargo xclippy` for development (`deny_warnings = false`).
503	///
504	/// # Errors
505	///
506	/// Returns [`Error::StepFailed`] if any clippy invocation exits non-zero,
507	/// or [`Error::Io`] if `crates/ars-i18n/Cargo.toml` cannot be read.
508	pub fn clippy_workspace(message_format: Option<&str>, deny_warnings: bool) -> Result<(), Error> {
509	    // Exclude ars-i18n: its `icu4x` and `web-intl` features are mutually
510	    // exclusive, so `--all-features` would trigger a compile_error!.
511	    // Instead, lint ars-i18n twice — once per backend — to cover all features.
512	    let mut workspace_args = vec![
513	        "clippy",
514	        "--workspace",
515	        "--all-targets",
516	        "--all-features",
517	        "--exclude",
518	        "ars-i18n",
519	    ];
520	
521	    if deny_warnings {
522	        workspace_args.extend_from_slice(&["--", "-D", "warnings"]);
523	    }
524	
525	    cargo_with_format(Step::Clippy, &workspace_args, message_format)?;
526	
527	    let [icu4x_features, web_intl_features] = i18n::i18n_feature_lists().map_err(Error::Io)?;
528	
529	    for features in [&icu4x_features, &web_intl_features] {
530	        let mut args = vec![
531	            "clippy",
532	            "-p",
533	            "ars-i18n",
534	            "--all-targets",
535	            "--no-default-features",
536	            "--features",
537	            features.as_str(),
538	        ];
539	
540	        if deny_warnings {
541	            args.extend_from_slice(&["--", "-D", "warnings"]);
542	        }
543	
544	        cargo_with_format(Step::Clippy, &args, message_format)?;
545	    }
546	
547	    Ok(())
548	}

607	        dioxus_test_dir: PathBuf::from("crates/ars-dioxus/tests"),

619	fn run_snapshot_count() -> Result<(), Error> {
620	    match lint::check_snapshot_count(&lint::SnapshotCountOptions {
621	        snapshots_dir: PathBuf::from("crates"),
622	        min_per_variant: 3,
623	        // Soft per-component budget formula:
624	        //
625	        //     budget = min(per_part_per_variant × variants × parts,
626	        //                  max_per_component)
627	        //
628	        // - `per_part_per_variant: 3` matches the
629	        //   `spec/testing/13-policies.md` §3.1 review guideline.
630	        // - `max_per_component: 40` is the hard ceiling that protects
631	        //   review-time fatigue regardless of anatomy size. Components
632	        //   with rich anatomy (Dialog, Popover) hit it; components with
633	        //   small anatomy (Tooltip, Presence) are gated by the formula.
634	        per_part_per_variant: 3,
635	        max_per_component: 40,
636	    }) {
637	        Ok(output) => {
638	            eprint!("{output}");
639	            Ok(())
640	        }
641	
642	        Err(error) => Err(Error::Lint(error)),
643	    }
644	}

646	fn run_spec_compile_snippets() -> Result<(), Error> {
647	    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().map_err(Error::Io)?;
648	
649	    let root = manifest::SpecRoot::discover(&cwd).map_err(Error::Spec)?;
650	
651	    // `fix = false` — CI must never silently rewrite spec files. Maintainers
652	    // run `cargo xtask spec compile-snippets --fix` locally to apply fixes.
653	    let report = spec::compile_snippets::execute(&root, false).map_err(Error::Spec)?;
654	
655	    if report.contains("finding(s) across") {
656	        eprint!("{report}");
657	
658	        return Err(Error::StepFailed {
659	            step: Step::SpecCompileSnippets,
660	            command: "cargo xtask spec compile-snippets".to_string(),
661	            code: Some(1),
662	        });
663	    }
664	
665	    eprint!("{report}");
666	
667	    Ok(())
668	}

1	//! Mutation-testing orchestration via [`cargo-mutants`](https://mutants.rs).
2	//!
3	//! Powers `cargo xmutants`, applying the same per-crate `--features` profile
4	//! as the Nightly CI mutation matrix so local runs do not fail on snapshot or
5	//! i18n-gated code paths that plain `cargo mutants` misses.
6	
7	use std::{
8	    fmt::{self, Display},
9	    io,
10	    path::PathBuf,
11	    process::{self, Stdio},
12	};
13	
14	/// Pinned `cargo-mutants` version — keep in sync with `nightly.yml` and
15	/// `AGENTS.md`.
16	pub const CARGO_MUTANTS_VERSION: &str = "27.0.0";
17	
18	/// Options for a local mutation-testing run.
19	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
20	pub struct Options {
21	    /// Crate package name (for example `ars-components`).
22	    pub package: String,
23	
24	    /// Optional source file limiting the mutation set.
25	    pub file: Option<PathBuf>,
26	
27	    /// When `true`, list mutants without executing tests.
28	    pub list: bool,
29	
30	    /// Optional shard selector (`k/n`, zero-based `k`).
31	    pub shard: Option<String>,
32	
33	    /// Output directory forwarded to `cargo mutants --output`.
34	    pub output: PathBuf,
35	
36	    /// Override the workspace default feature string for this package.
37	    pub features: Option<String>,
38	}
39	
40	/// Errors returned by `cargo xmutants`.
41	#[derive(Debug)]
42	pub enum Error {
43	    /// `cargo-mutants` is not installed or not on `PATH`.
44	    MissingTool {
45	        /// Human-readable tool name.
46	        tool: String,
47	
48	        /// Suggested install command.
49	        install_hint: String,
50	    },
51	
52	    /// The subprocess exited unsuccessfully.
53	    CommandFailed {
54	        /// Exit code, if available.
55	        code: Option<i32>,
56	    },
57	
58	    /// IO error while spawning the subprocess.
59	    Io(io::Error),
60	}
61	
62	impl Display for Error {
63	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
64	        match self {
65	            Self::MissingTool { tool, install_hint } => {
66	                write!(
67	                    f,
68	                    "missing required tool: {tool}\n  install: {install_hint}"
69	                )
70	            }
71	
72	            Self::CommandFailed { code } => {
73	                write!(f, "cargo mutants failed")?;
74	
75	                if let Some(code) = code {
76	                    write!(f, " (exit {code})")?;
77	                }
78	
79	                Ok(())
80	            }
81	
82	            Self::Io(err) => err.fmt(f),
83	        }
84	    }
85	}
86	
87	impl From<io::Error> for Error {
88	    fn from(value: io::Error) -> Self {
89	        Self::Io(value)
90	    }
91	}
92	
93	/// Returns the default `--features` string for `package`, matching the Nightly
94	/// mutation-testing matrix. `None` means no `--features` flag is forwarded.
95	#[must_use]
96	pub fn default_features(package: &str) -> Option<&'static str> {
97	    match package {
98	        "ars-components" => Some("ars-components/i18n"),
99	        _ => None,
100	    }
101	}
102	
103	/// Runs `cargo mutants` with workspace-aligned feature flags.
104	///
105	/// # Errors
106	///
107	/// Returns [`Error::MissingTool`] when `cargo-mutants` is unavailable,
108	/// [`Error::CommandFailed`] when the subprocess exits non-zero, or
109	/// [`Error::Io`] on spawn failures.
110	pub fn run(options: &Options) -> Result<(), Error> {
111	    preflight_cargo_mutants()?;
112	
113	    let mut command = process::Command::new("cargo");
114	    command
115	        .arg("mutants")
116	        .arg("-p")
117	        .arg(&options.package)
118	        .arg("--output")
119	        .arg(&options.output);
120	
121	    if let Some(file) = &options.file {
122	        command.arg("-f").arg(file);
123	    }
124	
125	    if options.list {
126	        command.arg("--list");
127	    }
128	
129	    if let Some(shard) = &options.shard {
130	        command.arg("--shard").arg(shard);
131	    }
132	
133	    let features = options
134	        .features
135	        .as_deref()
136	        .or_else(|| default_features(&options.package));
137	
138	    if let Some(features) = features
139	        && !features.is_empty()
140	    {
141	        command.arg("--features").arg(features);
142	    }
143	
144	    command.stdin(Stdio::inherit());
145	    command.stdout(Stdio::inherit());
146	    command.stderr(Stdio::inherit());
147	
148	    let status = command.status()?;
149	
150	    if status.success() {
151	        Ok(())
152	    } else {
153	        Err(Error::CommandFailed {
154	            code: status.code(),
155	        })
156	    }
157	}
158	
159	fn preflight_cargo_mutants() -> Result<(), Error> {
160	    let output = process::Command::new("cargo")
161	        .args(["mutants", "--version"])
162	        .output()?;
163	
164	    if output.status.success() {
165	        return Ok(());
166	    }
167	
168	    Err(Error::MissingTool {
169	        tool: "cargo-mutants".into(),
170	        install_hint: format!(
171	            "cargo install cargo-mutants --locked --version {CARGO_MUTANTS_VERSION}"
172	        ),
173	    })
174	}
175	
176	#[cfg(test)]
177	mod tests {
178	    use super::*;
179	
180	    #[test]
181	    fn default_features_matches_nightly_matrix() {
182	        assert_eq!(
183	            default_features("ars-components"),
184	            Some("ars-components/i18n")
185	        );
186	        assert_eq!(default_features("ars-collections"), None);
187	        assert_eq!(default_features("ars-core"), None);
188	        assert_eq!(default_features("ars-a11y"), None);
189	        assert_eq!(default_features("ars-forms"), None);
190	        assert_eq!(default_features("ars-interactions"), None);
191	    }
192	
193	    #[test]
194	    fn cargo_mutants_version_matches_nightly_pin() {
195	        assert_eq!(CARGO_MUTANTS_VERSION, "27.0.0");
196	    }
197	}
198

744

757	            "--no-fail-fast",

760	
761	    let mut reports = vec![native_lcov];
762	
763	    for target in coverage::default_wasm_coverage_targets() {

765	
766	        coverage::generate_wasm_lcov(&coverage::WasmCoverageOptions {
767	            package: target.package.to_owned(),
768	            output: wasm_lcov.clone(),

796	fn run_test_stage(step: Step, stage: test::Stage) -> Result<(), Error> {
797	    test::run_stage(stage)
798	        .map(|_| ())
799	        .map_err(|error| map_test_error(step, error))
800	}
801	
802	pub(crate) fn map_test_error(step: Step, error: test::Error) -> Error {
803	    match error {
804	        test::Error::MissingTool { tool, install_hint } => {
805	            Error::MissingTool { tool, install_hint }
806	        }
807	
808	        test::Error::CommandFailed { command, code } => Error::StepFailed {
809	            step,
810	            command,
811	            code,
812	        },
813	
814	        test::Error::Io(error) => Error::Io(error),
815	
816	        test::Error::Failed { summary } => Error::Io(io::Error::other(summary)),
817	    }
818	}
819	
820	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
821	// Subprocess helper
822	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
823	
824	/// Run `cargo <args>` with an optional `--message-format` flag injected.
825	///
826	/// The flag is inserted before `--` if present, otherwise appended. This
827	/// lets rust-analyzer's `overrideCommand` request JSON diagnostics via
828	/// `cargo xtask ci clippy --message-format=json`.
829	fn cargo_with_format(step: Step, args: &[&str], message_format: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), Error> {
830	    if let Some(fmt) = message_format {
831	        let fmt_flag = format!("--message-format={fmt}");
832	
833	        let mut full_args = Vec::with_capacity(args.len() + 1);
834	
835	        let mut inserted = false;
836	
837	        for &arg in args {
838	            if arg == "--" && !inserted {
839	                full_args.push(fmt_flag.as_str());
840	                inserted = true;
841	            }
842	            full_args.push(arg);
843	        }
844	
845	        if !inserted {
846	            full_args.push(fmt_flag.as_str());
847	        }
848	
849	        cargo(step, &full_args)
850	    } else {
851	        cargo(step, args)
852	    }
853	}
854	
855	/// Run `cargo <args>`, inheriting stdout/stderr.
856	///
857	/// Returns `Ok(())` on exit-code 0, or `CiError::StepFailed` otherwise.
858	pub(crate) fn cargo(step: Step, args: &[&str]) -> Result<(), Error> {
859	    let display_cmd = format!("cargo {}", args.join(" "));
860	
861	    eprintln!("  > {display_cmd}");
862	
863	    let status = process::Command::new("cargo")
864	        .args(args)
865	        .status()
866	        .map_err(Error::Io)?;
867	
868	    if status.success() {
869	        Ok(())
870	    } else {
871	        Err(Error::StepFailed {
872	            step,
873	            command: display_cmd,
874	            code: status.code(),
875	        })
876	    }
877	}
878	
879	/// Run `leptosfmt --check` over Leptos adapter and example code.
880	fn leptosfmt_check() -> Result<(), Error> {
881	    let inputs = leptosfmt_inputs()?;
882	
883	    let mut args = Vec::with_capacity(3 + inputs.len());
884	
885	    args.push("--check");
886	    args.push("--experimental-tailwind");
887	    args.push("--quiet");
888	    args.extend(inputs.iter().map(String::as_str));
889	
890	    run_leptosfmt(&args)
891	}

> AGENT

I have everything I need. Before writing the plan, one critical thing the chosen "hard fail above threshold" mode forces me to flag:

- **`cargo crap --workspace` scores every workspace member against `lcov.info`.** `ars-derive` (proc-macro, intentionally unmeasured) and any non-instrumented source would be scored at 0% coverage (`--missing pessimistic` default) and explode the CRAP score, failing CI for the wrong reasons. So the gate needs scoped excludes, and — because we can't run the ~90-min coverage job in plan mode — a **calibration pass during implementation** to confirm real code passes threshold 30 before we lock the gate. The plan builds that in.
- **Removing the `check` job** means GitHub branch-protection "required status checks" must be updated by a repo admin, or merges will hang waiting for a job that no longer exists. I'll flag this as a required manual follow-up.

Let me write the plan.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.claude/plans/i-want-to-add-binary-squid.md",
  "content": "# Integrate cargo-crap + improve CI\n\n## Context\n\nWe want two things in one effort:\n\n1. **Integrate [`cargo-crap`](https://minikin.me/blog/cargo-crap)** (the CRAP metric: cyclomatic\n   complexity vs. test coverage) into the xtask CI engine and GitHub Actions. cargo-crap surfaces\n   functions that are both complex and undertested — the highest-risk code to modify, especially\n   under AI-assisted development. It is already installed locally at `~/.cargo/bin/cargo-crap`\n   (v0.2.2).\n\n2. **Speed up CI and remove redundancy.** The `check` job is redundant — `clippy --workspace\n   --all-targets --all-features` compiles a strict superset of what `cargo check` compiles, plus\n   lints. And the whole pipeline serializes behind `fmt → clippy → unit` before fanning out, even\n   though most fan-out jobs don't consume unit-test *results* (clippy already guarantees the code\n   compiles).\n\n**Decisions taken (user-confirmed):**\n- cargo-crap gates CI with a **hard fail**: `cargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info --fail-above\n  --threshold 30`.\n- **Remove `check` entirely** (variant, `run_check`, CI job, pipeline entries; rewire dependents).\n- **Moderate CI speedup**: rewire independent compile jobs from `needs: [unit]` to `needs:\n  [clippy]` so they run in parallel with `unit` instead of waiting for it.\n\n**Key facts established during exploration:**\n- xtask is the single source of truth for CI steps (`xtask/src/ci/mod.rs`). CI YAML just calls\n  `cargo xtask ci <step>`.\n- The `coverage` step (`run_coverage`, `mod.rs:730`) already writes the merged native+wasm\n  `lcov.info` to the repo root (`mod.rs:743,782`). cargo-crap consumes exactly that file and needs\n  **no recompile** — it parses source + reads lcov. So cargo-crap belongs right after `Coverage`\n  in the pipeline and as an appended step in the existing `coverage` CI job (zero extra compile,\n  no artifact round-trip).\n- `cargo-crap` v0.2.2 takes options directly (`cargo crap [OPTIONS]`, no `crap` subcommand);\n  `--fail-above` controls exit code independently of `--format`; `--format github` emits\n  `::warning` annotations; it auto-detects `--repo-url`/`--commit-ref` from `GITHUB_SERVER_URL`/\n  `GITHUB_REPOSITORY`/`GITHUB_SHA`.\n- The existing tool-integration template to mirror is `xtask/src/mutants.rs` (pinned-version const,\n  `Options` struct, `preflight_*` check, `MissingTool` error, unit tests asserting the version pin\n  and argument construction).\n- `ci/mod.rs` has a thorough inline test suite (`mod tests`, ~line 1275) asserting pipeline\n  contents, `step_name` coverage, and fast-vs-full invariants. These tests must be updated in lockstep.\n\n## Part A — cargo-crap integration\n\n### A1. New module `xtask/src/crap.rs` (mirror `mutants.rs`)\n\nCentralize all gate config here as testable constants (single source of truth, same pattern as\n`CARGO_MUTANTS_VERSION` / `LEPTOSFMT_VERSION`):\n\n- `pub const CARGO_CRAP_VERSION: &str = \"0.2.2\";` — keep in sync with `ci.yml` and CLAUDE.md.\n- `pub const CRAP_THRESHOLD: u32 = 30;` — the fail-above threshold.\n- `const EXCLUDE_GLOBS: &[&str]` — files skipped at walk time. **Seed with** `crates/ars-derive/**`\n  (proc-macro crate, intentionally unmeasured by llvm-cov; would otherwise score 0% coverage and\n  explode CRAP). Add `target/**` defensively. Each entry carries a justification comment.\n- `const ALLOW_GLOBS: &[&str]` — escape hatch for genuinely-justified high-CRAP functions\n  discovered during calibration (A4). Empty initially. Each entry **must** have a comment\n  explaining why (matches the repo's \"named escape hatch + justification\" convention, cf.\n  `mutants.toml` `[[skip]]`).\n- `pub enum Format { Human, Github }` + `fn format_for_env() -> Format` reading `GITHUB_ACTIONS`\n  (set by Actions) → `Github`, else `Human`. Keeps CI annotations without polluting local output.\n- `pub struct Options { lcov: PathBuf, threshold: u32, fail_above: bool, format: Format }`.\n- `fn build_args(opts: &Options) -> Vec<String>` — **pure, testable** arg builder producing\n  `[\"crap\", \"--workspace\", \"--lcov\", <lcov>, \"--threshold\", <n>, \"--format\", <f>, (\"--fail-above\"),\n  \"--exclude\", <g>..., \"--allow\", <g>...]`.\n- `pub fn run(opts: &Options) -> Result<(), Error>` — `preflight_cargo_crap()`, then preflight that\n  `opts.lcov` exists (helpful error pointing at `cargo xci coverage`), spawn `cargo` with\n  `build_args`, inherit stdio, map exit code.\n- `preflight_cargo_crap()` — `cargo crap --version` succeeds, else `Error::MissingTool` with hint\n  `cargo install cargo-crap --locked --version {CARGO_CRAP_VERSION}`.\n- `Error` enum + `Display` + `From<io::Error>` — copy the shape from `mutants.rs`.\n- `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`: assert `CARGO_CRAP_VERSION == \"0.2.2\"`; assert `build_args` includes\n  `--workspace`, `--fail-above` when set and omits it when not, the threshold, every `EXCLUDE_GLOBS`\n  entry as an `--exclude`, every `ALLOW_GLOBS` entry as an `--allow`; assert `format_for_env` maps\n  correctly given an injected env value (refactor to take the var so it's testable).\n\nRegister `mod crap;` in `xtask/src/lib.rs` and `main.rs`.\n\n### A2. Wire `Step::Crap` into `xtask/src/ci/mod.rs`\n\n- Add `Step::Crap` variant (doc: \"Run cargo-crap CRAP gate over the merged lcov.info\").\n- `step_name`: `Step::Crap => \"crap\"` (`mod.rs:1207`).\n- `run_step`: `Step::Crap => run_crap()` (`mod.rs:393`).\n- `fn run_crap()` → `crap::run(&crap::Options { lcov: \"lcov.info\".into(), threshold:\n  crap::CRAP_THRESHOLD, fail_above: true, format: crap::format_for_env() })`, mapping `crap::Error`\n  into `ci::Error` (extend `ci::Error` with a `Crap(crap::Error)` arm + `Display`, mirroring the\n  existing `Coverage`/`Lint` arms).\n- Add `Step::Crap` to `PIPELINE_ORDER` **immediately after `Step::Coverage`** (`mod.rs:138`) so it\n  reads the freshly merged lcov. **Do not** add to `FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER` (fast skips coverage → no\n  lcov).\n\n### A3. The plan is written. Here's the summary.

## Plan: integrate cargo-crap + speed up CI

**cargo-crap (hard-fail gate):**
- New `xtask/src/crap.rs` modeled on the existing `mutants.rs` — pinned `CARGO_CRAP_VERSION = "0.2.2"`, `CRAP_THRESHOLD = 30`, testable `build_args`, preflight, `EXCLUDE_GLOBS`/`ALLOW_GLOBS` (justified-escape-hatch list), and `format_for_env` so CI gets `::warning` annotations while local output stays human-readable.
- New `Step::Crap` placed right after `Step::Coverage` in the full pipeline (it reuses the merged `lcov.info` that `run_coverage` already writes — **zero recompile**), excluded from the fast pipeline. Appended as a step in the existing `coverage` CI job rather than a separate job.
- Dev entrypoint `cargo xcrap` for local reproduction.

**CI cleanup (your confirmed choices):**
- Remove `check` entirely (clippy `--all-targets` is a strict superset) — variant, `run_check`, CI job, and dependents rewired.
- Moderate speedup: independent compile jobs move from `needs: [unit]` to `needs: [clippy]`, removing a full compile stage from the critical path.

**Two things I flagged that need your awareness:**
1. **Calibration is mandatory before the gate locks.** `cargo crap --workspace` scores every member against `lcov.info`; `ars-derive` (unmeasured proc-macro) would falsely explode, so it's seeded into `EXCLUDE_GLOBS`. I can't run the ~90-min coverage job in plan mode, so during implementation I'll do a real coverage→crap pass and surface any over-threshold functions to you before finalizing (tests preferred over allow-list over threshold change).
2. **Branch protection** must drop the "Workspace Check" required status after removing the `check` job — a GitHub-settings action only you (admin) can do, or merges will hang. Called out in the plan and to go in the PR body.

The user doesn't want to proceed with this tool use. The tool use was rejected (eg. if it was a file edit, the new_string was NOT written to the file). To tell you how to proceed, the user said:
Should we keep a unit tests step if we also have a coverage step? Actual question

Note: The user's next message may contain a correction or preference. Pay close attention — if they explain what went wrong or how they'd prefer you to work, consider saving that to memory for future sessions.
```

> AGENT

Good question — and the answer is **keep `unit`**. They overlap in test *execution* but serve different purposes, and after the moderate rewiring they're no longer serially redundant.

What each actually does:

| | `unit` job | `coverage` job |
|---|---|---|
| Command | `cargo xtest unit` + `cargo insta test --unreferenced=reject` | `cargo +nightly llvm-cov nextest --branch --workspace` + wasm coverage + threshold check (+ crap) |
| Toolchain | **stable** (what users actually build with) | **nightly** (llvm-cov `--branch` needs it) |
| Speed | fast, no instrumentation | the ~90-min tail |
| Snapshot gate | **yes** (`--unreferenced=reject`) | no |

Why dropping `unit` would be a regression:

1. **Feedback latency.** `coverage` is the slowest job in the pipeline. If it's the only thing running tests, every test break now takes ~90 min to surface instead of a few minutes. `unit` is the fast fail signal.
2. **Stable vs nightly.** `unit` runs the suite on the stable toolchain — the real correctness signal for the toolchain users ship on. `coverage` runs on nightly purely to get instrumentation. A divergence between them is exactly the kind of thing you want stable CI to catch.
3. **Snapshot enforcement.** `unit` also runs `cargo insta test --unreferenced=reject`, which `coverage` doesn't. That gate would be lost.

On the redundancy you're sensing: in the *current* graph it's real-ish — `coverage` runs `needs: [unit]`, so it executes the tests a *second* time, serially, after `unit` already ran them. My moderate rewiring fixes exactly that: both become `needs: [clippy]` and run **in parallel**. The suite still executes twice, but concurrently on separate runners, so it costs runner-minutes, not wall-clock — and you keep the fast stable signal + snapshot gate while coverage does its slow instrumented measurement alongside.

So no plan change needed — the rewiring already converts the serial double-run into a parallel one, which is the win here. If you'd instead prefer to *reduce runner cost* by having `coverage` not duplicate the native test run, that's a different (and fiddlier) optimization we could fold in, but it trades feedback latency for runner-minutes and I wouldn't recommend it.

Want me to proceed with the plan as written, or fold in that runner-cost optimization first?

> DEVELOPER

The user doesn't want to proceed with this tool use. The tool use was rejected (eg. if it was a file edit, the new_string was NOT written to the file). To tell you how to proceed, the user said:
30 is the recommended threshold?

Note: The user's next message may contain a correction or preference. Pay close attention — if they explain what went wrong or how they'd prefer you to work, consider saving that to memory for future sessions.
```

> AGENT

Yes — 30 is the recommended starting threshold, on three grounds:

1. **It's cargo-crap's built-in default** (`--threshold` defaults to 30).
2. **It's the canonical CRAP cutoff** from the metric's origin (Crap4J, Savoia 2007), which is where the tool inherits it.
3. **The blog's own CI example uses `--threshold 30`.**

What 30 actually means, given `CRAP = complexity² · (1 − coverage)³ + complexity`:

| Function | CRAP | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| complexity 30, **100% covered** | 30 | passes (boundary) |
| complexity 20, 80% covered | 23.2 | passes |
| complexity 10, 50% covered | 22.5 | passes |
| complexity 6, **0% covered** | 42 | **fails** |
| complexity 10, 0% covered | 110 | **fails** |

So it's lenient on well-tested code (full coverage tolerates up to ~30 branches) and strict on complex-but-untested code — exactly the risk profile we want to catch.

The one caveat for *this* repo: state-machine transition functions are match-heavy and can carry high cyclomatic complexity. But they're also heavily tested (74–77%+ coverage thresholds, proptest), which crushes the CRAP score back down. Whether 30 holds in practice is precisely what the **mandatory calibration pass** (A5) verifies against the real `lcov.info` — if a well-tested state machine needs headroom, we bump the threshold or add a justified allow-entry then, with real numbers, rather than guessing now.

So 30 stays as the documented starting point; calibration confirms or adjusts it. No plan change needed.

## Approved Plan:
# Integrate cargo-crap + improve CI

## Context

We want two things in one effort:

1. **Integrate [`cargo-crap`](https://minikin.me/blog/cargo-crap)** (the CRAP metric: cyclomatic
   complexity vs. test coverage) into the xtask CI engine and GitHub Actions. cargo-crap surfaces
   functions that are both complex and undertested — the highest-risk code to modify, especially
   under AI-assisted development. It is already installed locally at `~/.cargo/bin/cargo-crap`
   (v0.2.2).

2. **Speed up CI and remove redundancy.** The `check` job is redundant — `clippy --workspace
   --all-targets --all-features` compiles a strict superset of what `cargo check` compiles, plus
   lints. And the whole pipeline serializes behind `fmt → clippy → unit` before fanning out, even
   though most fan-out jobs don't consume unit-test *results* (clippy already guarantees the code
   compiles).

**Decisions taken (user-confirmed):**
- cargo-crap gates CI with a **hard fail**: `cargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info --fail-above
  --threshold 30`.
- **Remove `check` entirely** (variant, `run_check`, CI job, pipeline entries; rewire dependents).
- **Moderate CI speedup**: rewire independent compile jobs from `needs: [unit]` to `needs:
  [clippy]` so they run in parallel with `unit` instead of waiting for it.

**Key facts established during exploration:**
- xtask is the single source of truth for CI steps (`xtask/src/ci/mod.rs`). CI YAML just calls
  `cargo xtask ci <step>`.
- The `coverage` step (`run_coverage`, `mod.rs:730`) already writes the merged native+wasm
  `lcov.info` to the repo root (`mod.rs:743,782`). cargo-crap consumes exactly that file and needs
  **no recompile** — it parses source + reads lcov. So cargo-crap belongs right after `Coverage`
  in the pipeline and as an appended step in the existing `coverage` CI job (zero extra compile,
  no artifact round-trip).
- `cargo-crap` v0.2.2 takes options directly (`cargo crap [OPTIONS]`, no `crap` subcommand);
  `--fail-above` controls exit code independently of `--format`; `--format github` emits
  `::warning` annotations; it auto-detects `--repo-url`/`--commit-ref` from `GITHUB_SERVER_URL`/
  `GITHUB_REPOSITORY`/`GITHUB_SHA`.
- The existing tool-integration template to mirror is `xtask/src/mutants.rs` (pinned-version const,
  `Options` struct, `preflight_*` check, `MissingTool` error, unit tests asserting the version pin
  and argument construction).
- `ci/mod.rs` has a thorough inline test suite (`mod tests`, ~line 1275) asserting pipeline
  contents, `step_name` coverage, and fast-vs-full invariants. These tests must be updated in lockstep.

## Part A — cargo-crap integration

### A1. New module `xtask/src/crap.rs` (mirror `mutants.rs`)

Centralize all gate config here as testable constants (single source of truth, same pattern as
`CARGO_MUTANTS_VERSION` / `LEPTOSFMT_VERSION`):

- `pub const CARGO_CRAP_VERSION: &str = "0.2.2";` — keep in sync with `ci.yml` and CLAUDE.md.
- `pub const CRAP_THRESHOLD: u32 = 30;` — the fail-above threshold.
- `const EXCLUDE_GLOBS: &[&str]` — files skipped at walk time. **Seed with** `crates/ars-derive/**`
  (proc-macro crate, intentionally unmeasured by llvm-cov; would otherwise score 0% coverage and
  explode CRAP). Add `target/**` defensively. Each entry carries a justification comment.
- `const ALLOW_GLOBS: &[&str]` — escape hatch for genuinely-justified high-CRAP functions
  discovered during calibration (A4). Empty initially. Each entry **must** have a comment
  explaining why (matches the repo's "named escape hatch + justification" convention, cf.
  `mutants.toml` `[[skip]]`).
- `pub enum Format { Human, Github }` + `fn format_for_env() -> Format` reading `GITHUB_ACTIONS`
  (set by Actions) → `Github`, else `Human`. Keeps CI annotations without polluting local output.
- `pub struct Options { lcov: PathBuf, threshold: u32, fail_above: bool, format: Format }`.
- `fn build_args(opts: &Options) -> Vec<String>` — **pure, testable** arg builder producing
  `["crap", "--workspace", "--lcov", <lcov>, "--threshold", <n>, "--format", <f>, ("--fail-above"),
  "--exclude", <g>..., "--allow", <g>...]`.
- `pub fn run(opts: &Options) -> Result<(), Error>` — `preflight_cargo_crap()`, then preflight that
  `opts.lcov` exists (helpful error pointing at `cargo xci coverage`), spawn `cargo` with
  `build_args`, inherit stdio, map exit code.
- `preflight_cargo_crap()` — `cargo crap --version` succeeds, else `Error::MissingTool` with hint
  `cargo install cargo-crap --locked --version {CARGO_CRAP_VERSION}`.
- `Error` enum + `Display` + `From<io::Error>` — copy the shape from `mutants.rs`.
- `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`: assert `CARGO_CRAP_VERSION == "0.2.2"`; assert `build_args` includes
  `--workspace`, `--fail-above` when set and omits it when not, the threshold, every `EXCLUDE_GLOBS`
  entry as an `--exclude`, every `ALLOW_GLOBS` entry as an `--allow`; assert `format_for_env` maps
  correctly given an injected env value (refactor to take the var so it's testable).

Register `mod crap;` in `xtask/src/lib.rs` and `main.rs`.

### A2. Wire `Step::Crap` into `xtask/src/ci/mod.rs`

- Add `Step::Crap` variant (doc: "Run cargo-crap CRAP gate over the merged lcov.info").
- `step_name`: `Step::Crap => "crap"` (`mod.rs:1207`).
- `run_step`: `Step::Crap => run_crap()` (`mod.rs:393`).
- `fn run_crap()` → `crap::run(&crap::Options { lcov: "lcov.info".into(), threshold:
  crap::CRAP_THRESHOLD, fail_above: true, format: crap::format_for_env() })`, mapping `crap::Error`
  into `ci::Error` (extend `ci::Error` with a `Crap(crap::Error)` arm + `Display`, mirroring the
  existing `Coverage`/`Lint` arms).
- Add `Step::Crap` to `PIPELINE_ORDER` **immediately after `Step::Coverage`** (`mod.rs:138`) so it
  reads the freshly merged lcov. **Do not** add to `FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER` (fast skips coverage → no
  lcov).

### A3. Update inline tests + docs in `ci/mod.rs` and `main.rs`

- `step_name_covers_all_variants` (`mod.rs:1418`): add `Step::Crap` to the `all` array.
- Add a test: `Step::Crap` is in `PIPELINE_ORDER`, sits directly after `Step::Coverage`, and is
  **absent** from `FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER` (add `Step::Crap` to the `banned` list in
  `fast_pipeline_excludes_*` since it requires coverage's lcov).
- New dev entrypoint: add a `Crap { lcov: Option<PathBuf>, threshold: Option<u32> }` subcommand in
  `main.rs` → `crap::run(... format: Human ...)` (defaults `lcov.info`, `CRAP_THRESHOLD`), and a
  `xcrap = "xtask crap"` alias in `.cargo/config.toml`. Lets contributors reproduce the gate
  locally with readable output after a coverage run (parallels the `xclippy` dev vs `ci clippy`
  split).
- Update the "all 20 gates" prose (`main.rs:20`, `mod.rs:103`) and the `cargo xci check clippy`
  example in the module header (`mod.rs:5`) to drop `check` and mention `crap`.

### A4. CI: append cargo-crap to the existing `coverage` job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`)

In the `coverage` job (lines 218-288), after the `Coverage` step (line 269):
- Add `- uses: taiki-e/install-action@v2` with `tool: cargo-crap@0.2.2` (pinned; taiki-e falls
  back to cargo-binstall, which the blog confirms supports cargo-crap). Place it near the other
  `install-action` steps.
- Add `- name: CRAP gate \n run: cargo xtask ci crap`. It fails the job on threshold breach
  (`--fail-above`) and emits `::warning` annotations (github format via `format_for_env`). The
  existing `if: always()` Codecov upload still runs even if CRAP fails.

### A5. Calibration pass (MANDATORY during implementation, before locking the gate)

The full coverage job can't run in plan mode. During implementation:
1. Run `cargo xci coverage` locally (or the native-only subset) to produce a real `lcov.info`.
2. Run `cargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info --threshold 30 --format human --top 40` and inspect.
3. For every function over 30: prefer **adding tests** (lowers coverage-driven CRAP, matches the
   repo's test-first ethos); otherwise add a justified `EXCLUDE_GLOBS`/`ALLOW_GLOBS` entry. Only
   touch `CRAP_THRESHOLD` as a last resort.
4. **Surface the calibration result to the user** before finalizing — if real code can't pass 30
   cleanly, we decide together (more tests vs. allow-list vs. threshold) rather than silently
   widening the gate.

## Part B — CI speedup + remove `check`

### B1. Remove `check` from xtask

- Delete `Step::Check` variant (`mod.rs:37`), `step_name` arm (`mod.rs:1210`), `run_step` arm
  (`mod.rs:397`), `fn run_check` (`mod.rs:458`), and the `Step::Check` entry in `PIPELINE_ORDER`.
- Fix `explicit_steps_pass_through` test (`mod.rs:1287`, uses `Step::Check`) — switch to e.g.
  `[Step::Clippy, Step::Unit]`. Remove `Step::Check` from `step_name_covers_all_variants`.
- `cargo_with_format` stays (clippy still uses it). No other `run_check`-only code remains.

### B2. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` rewiring

- **Delete the `check` job** (lines 51-61).
- `unit.needs`: `[check, clippy]` → `[clippy]`.
- `mutual-exclusion.needs`: `[check]` → `[clippy]`.
- Rewire these jobs from `needs: [unit]` → `needs: [clippy]` (clippy guarantees compilation; none
  consume unit-test *results*): `i18n-browser`, `dom-browser`, `release`, `integration`, `adapter`,
  `error-variant-coverage`, `coverage`, `feature-flags-{core,i18n,subsystems,leptos,dioxus}`.
- `adapter-parity.needs`: `[unit, adapter]` → `[clippy]` (it only counts test files on disk).
- Net graph: `fmt → clippy → { unit, release, integration, adapter, adapter-parity,
  error-variant-coverage, mutual-exclusion, i18n-browser, dom-browser, coverage(+crap),
  feature-flags-* → feature-flag-check } `. Removes one full compile stage from the critical path.
- `feature-flag-check` aggregator unchanged.

### B3. Required manual follow-up (flag to user; cannot be done in-repo)

Removing the `check` job means GitHub **branch protection → required status checks** must drop
`Workspace Check` (job name "Workspace Check"), or PR merges will block waiting on a job that no
longer reports. This is a repo-admin action in GitHub settings, not a file change. Call it out in
the PR description.

## Critical files

- `xtask/src/crap.rs` — **new** module (mirror `xtask/src/mutants.rs`).
- `xtask/src/lib.rs`, `xtask/src/main.rs` — register module, add `Crap` subcommand, update prose.
- `xtask/src/ci/mod.rs` — add `Step::Crap` + `run_crap` + `Error::Crap`; remove `Step::Check` +
  `run_check`; update `PIPELINE_ORDER`, `step_name`, `run_step`, and the inline `mod tests`.
- `.cargo/config.toml` — add `xcrap = "xtask crap"`.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — remove `check` job; rewire `needs:`; append cargo-crap install +
  CRAP gate step to the `coverage` job.
- `CLAUDE.md` — short note: cargo-crap pinned to 0.2.2, blessed entrypoint `cargo xcrap` /
  `cargo xtask ci crap`, gate is `--fail-above --threshold 30`, allow-list lives in
  `crap.rs::ALLOW_GLOBS` with justifications. Mirror the existing cargo-mutants version-pin note.

## Reuse (do not reinvent)

- `xtask/src/mutants.rs` — copy its `Options`/`run`/`preflight`/`Error`/version-const/test shape.
- `ci::cargo()` helper (`mod.rs:858`) for spawning — but crap is a non-`cargo`-builtin tool invoked
  as `cargo crap`, so `crap::run` spawns `Command::new("cargo").arg("crap")...` with inherited
  stdio (same as `mutants::run`), not `ci::cargo` (which sets `Step`-typed errors).
- `coverage::*` already produces `lcov.info`; crap just reads it.

## Verification

1. `cargo test -p xtask` — inline `ci::tests` (pipeline contains `Crap` after `Coverage`, excludes
   it from fast, no `Check` anywhere) and `crap::tests` (version pin, `build_args`, format-for-env)
   all pass.
2. `cargo build -p xtask` — zero warnings (missing_docs etc.).
3. `cargo xcrap --help` and `cargo xtask ci crap` resolve and run.
4. **Calibration (A5):** real `cargo xci coverage` → `lcov.info` → `cargo xtask ci crap` exits 0
   with the seeded excludes (or surfaces offenders for a joint decision). This is the gate's
   acceptance test and must pass before the PR.
5. `cargo xci-fast` passes (confirms `check` removal didn't break the fast pipeline; confirms crap
   is correctly absent from fast).

   former `[unit]` dependent now `[clippy]`.
7. Push PR (with `Closes #<issue>` if issue-backed), note the branch-protection follow-up (B3) in
   the body, run the mandatory `post-implementation-audit` and `waiting-for-codex-review` skills per
   CLAUDE.md.

18	    /// Run CI pipeline steps locally (alias: `cargo xci`).
19	    ///
20	    /// `--profile full` (default) mirrors GitHub Actions exactly — all 20 gates,

22	    /// (~5 min): fmt, check, clippy, unit, integration, adapter,
23	    /// spec-compile-snippets, error-variant-coverage, mutual-exclusion,

38	        profile: ci::Profile,
39

44	    },
45	
46	    /// Format the workspace in place (alias: `cargo xfmt`).
