{
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui/xtask/src/crap.rs",
  "content": "//! CRAP-metric gate via [`cargo-crap`](https://crates.io/crates/cargo-crap).\n//!\n//! Powers `cargo xcrap` and the `crap` CI step. The CRAP score\n//! (`complexity² · (1 − coverage)³ + complexity`) flags functions that are both\n//! cyclomatically complex and poorly covered — the highest-risk code to modify.\n//! The gate reads the merged `lcov.info` that the `coverage` step already\n//! produces, so it never recompiles: it only parses source and the lcov report.\n\nuse std::{\n    fmt::{self, Display},\n    io,\n    path::{Path, PathBuf},\n    process::{self, Stdio},\n};\n\n/// Pinned `cargo-crap` version — keep in sync with `.github/workflows/ci.yml`\n/// and the CLAUDE.md note. Pinning is deliberate: cargo-crap's scoring and\n/// mutators can change between releases, and an unpinned install would silently\n/// shift the gate without a code change.\npub const CARGO_CRAP_VERSION: &str = \"0.2.2\";\n\n/// CRAP score above which a function fails the gate. Matches cargo-crap's\n/// built-in default and the canonical Crap4J cutoff: lenient on well-covered\n/// code (full coverage tolerates complexity up to this value), strict on\n/// complex-but-untested code.\npub const CRAP_THRESHOLD: u32 = 30;\n\n/// Files skipped at walk time (`--exclude`). Every entry must carry a\n/// justification so the gate's blind spots stay auditable.\nconst EXCLUDE_GLOBS: &[&str] = &[\n    // `ars-derive` is a proc-macro crate: its code runs inside the compiler, so\n    // `cargo-llvm-cov` never instruments it. Every function would otherwise be\n    // scored at 0% coverage (`--missing pessimistic`), exploding CRAP for code\n    // the coverage gate deliberately does not measure. It is exercised via\n    // `crates/ars-core/tests/derive_contract.rs`.\n    \"crates/ars-derive/**\",\n    // Build artifacts and generated output — never first-party code under test.\n    \"target/**\",\n];\n\n/// Functions suppressed from the report (`--allow`). Every entry must carry a\n/// justification. Prefer adding tests (which lowers the coverage-driven CRAP\n/// score) over adding an entry here.\nconst ALLOW_GLOBS: &[&str] = &[];\n\n/// Output format for the CRAP report.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum Format {\n    /// Human-readable ranked table — used for local development.\n    Human,\n\n    /// GitHub Actions workflow commands (`::warning` per crappy function) —\n    /// used in CI so offenders surface as PR annotations.\n    Github,\n}\n\nimpl Format {\n    /// The `--format` argument value cargo-crap expects.\n    const fn as_arg(self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            Self::Human => \"human\",\n            Self::Github => \"github\",\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Selects the report format from the `GITHUB_ACTIONS` environment variable,\n/// which GitHub Actions sets to `true` inside a workflow run.\n#[must_use]\npub fn format_for_env() -> Format {\n    format_from_ci_flag(std::env::var(\"GITHUB_ACTIONS\").ok().as_deref())\n}\n\n/// Maps the raw `GITHUB_ACTIONS` value to a [`Format`]. Split out from\n/// [`format_for_env`] so it is testable without mutating process environment.\nfn format_from_ci_flag(github_actions: Option<&str>) -> Format {\n    match github_actions {\n        Some(\"true\") => Format::Github,\n        _ => Format::Human,\n    }\n}\n\n/// Options for a CRAP gate run.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct Options {\n    /// Path to the merged lcov report (typically `lcov.info` at the repo root).\n    pub lcov: PathBuf,\n\n    /// CRAP score threshold forwarded as `--threshold`.\n    pub threshold: u32,\n\n    /// When `true`, pass `--fail-above` so the process exits non-zero if any\n    /// function exceeds [`Options::threshold`].\n    pub fail_above: bool,\n\n    /// Report output format.\n    pub format: Format,\n}\n\n/// Errors returned by the CRAP gate.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub enum Error {\n    /// `cargo-crap` is not installed or not on `PATH`.\n    MissingTool {\n        /// Human-readable tool name.\n        tool: String,\n\n        /// Suggested install command.\n        install_hint: String,\n    },\n\n    /// The lcov coverage report does not exist.\n    MissingLcov {\n        /// The path that was expected to hold the report.\n        path: PathBuf,\n    },\n\n    /// The subprocess exited unsuccessfully (a threshold breach under\n    /// `--fail-above`, or a cargo-crap error).\n    CommandFailed {\n        /// Exit code, if available.\n        code: Option<i32>,\n    },\n\n    /// IO error while spawning the subprocess.\n    Io(io::Error),\n}\n\nimpl Display for Error {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            Self::MissingTool { tool, install_hint } => {\n                write!(\n                    f,\n                    \"missing required tool: {tool}\\n  install: {install_hint}\"\n                )\n            }\n\n            Self::MissingLcov { path } => {\n                write!(\n                    f,\n                    \"coverage report not found: {}\\n  run `cargo xci coverage` first to generate it\",\n                    path.display()\n                )\n            }\n\n            Self::CommandFailed { code } => {\n                write!(f, \"cargo crap failed\")?;\n\n                if let Some(code) = code {\n                    write!(f, \" (exit {code})\")?;\n                }\n\n                Ok(())\n            }\n\n            Self::Io(err) => err.fmt(f),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl From<io::Error> for Error {\n    fn from(value: io::Error) -> Self {\n        Self::Io(value)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Builds the `cargo crap …` argument vector for `options`.\n///\n/// The leading `\"crap\"` is the subcommand name cargo injects when a binary is\n/// invoked as `cargo crap`; cargo-crap tolerates it. Split out as a pure\n/// function so the argument surface is unit-testable.\nfn build_args(options: &Options) -> Vec<String> {\n    let mut args = vec![\n        \"crap\".to_owned(),\n        \"--workspace\".to_owned(),\n        \"--lcov\".to_owned(),\n        options.lcov.display().to_string(),\n        \"--threshold\".to_owned(),\n        options.threshold.to_string(),\n        \"--format\".to_owned(),\n        options.format.as_arg().to_owned(),\n    ];\n\n    if options.fail_above {\n        args.push(\"--fail-above\".to_owned());\n    }\n\n    for glob in EXCLUDE_GLOBS {\n        args.push(\"--exclude\".to_owned());\n        args.push((*glob).to_owned());\n    }\n\n    for glob in ALLOW_GLOBS {\n        args.push(\"--allow\".to_owned());\n        args.push((*glob).to_owned());\n    }\n\n    args\n}\n\n/// Runs the CRAP gate.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// Returns [`Error::MissingTool`] when `cargo-crap` is unavailable,\n/// [`Error::MissingLcov`] when the coverage report is absent,\n/// [`Error::CommandFailed`] when the subprocess exits non-zero (including a\n/// threshold breach under `--fail-above`), or [`Error::Io`] on spawn failures.\npub fn run(options: &Options) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    preflight_cargo_crap()?;\n    preflight_lcov(&options.lcov)?;\n\n    let args = build_args(options);\n\n    eprintln!(\"  > cargo {}\", args.join(\" \"));\n\n    let status = process::Command::new(\"cargo\")\n        .args(&args)\n        .stdin(Stdio::inherit())\n        .stdout(Stdio::inherit())\n        .stderr(Stdio::inherit())\n        .status()?;\n\n    if status.success() {\n        Ok(())\n    } else {\n        Err(Error::CommandFailed {\n            code: status.code(),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\nfn preflight_cargo_crap() -> Result<(), Error> {\n    let output = process::Command::new(\"cargo\")\n        .args([\"crap\", \"--version\"])\n        .output()?;\n\n    if output.status.success() {\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n\n    Err(Error::MissingTool {\n        tool: \"cargo-crap\".into(),\n        install_hint: format!(\"cargo install cargo-crap --locked --version {CARGO_CRAP_VERSION}\"),\n    })\n}\n\nfn preflight_lcov(lcov: &Path) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    if lcov.exists() {\n        Ok(())\n    } else {\n        Err(Error::MissingLcov {\n            path: lcov.to_path_buf(),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    fn options(fail_above: bool, format: Format) -> Options {\n        Options {\n            lcov: PathBuf::from(\"lcov.info\"),\n            threshold: CRAP_THRESHOLD,\n            fail_above,\n            format,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Collect the values that follow every occurrence of `flag` in `args`.\n    fn values_after<'a>(args: &'a [String], flag: &str) -> Vec<&'a str> {\n        args.windows(2)\n            .filter(|pair| pair[0] == flag)\n            .map(|pair| pair[1].as_str())\n            .collect()\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn cargo_crap_version_matches_ci_pin() {\n        assert_eq!(CARGO_CRAP_VERSION, \"0.2.2\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn threshold_is_thirty() {\n        assert_eq!(CRAP_THRESHOLD, 30);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn build_args_starts_with_crap_subcommand_and_workspace() {\n        let args = build_args(&options(true, Format::Github));\n\n        assert_eq!(args[0], \"crap\");\n        assert!(args.iter().any(|arg| arg == \"--workspace\"));\n        assert_eq!(values_after(&args, \"--lcov\"), vec![\"lcov.info\"]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn build_args_carries_threshold_and_format() {\n        let args = build_args(&options(false, Format::Human));\n\n        assert_eq!(values_after(&args, \"--threshold\"), vec![\"30\"]);\n        assert_eq!(values_after(&args, \"--format\"), vec![\"human\"]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn build_args_toggles_fail_above() {\n        assert!(\n            build_args(&options(true, Format::Github))\n                .iter()\n                .any(|arg| arg == \"--fail-above\")\n        );\n\n        assert!(\n            !build_args(&options(false, Format::Github))\n                .iter()\n                .any(|arg| arg == \"--fail-above\")\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn build_args_forwards_every_exclude_and_allow_glob() {\n        let args = build_args(&options(true, Format::Github));\n\n        let excludes = values_after(&args, \"--exclude\");\n        for glob in EXCLUDE_GLOBS {\n            assert!(excludes.contains(glob), \"missing --exclude {glob}\");\n        }\n        assert_eq!(excludes.len(), EXCLUDE_GLOBS.len());\n\n        let allows = values_after(&args, \"--allow\");\n        for glob in ALLOW_GLOBS {\n            assert!(allows.contains(glob), \"missing --allow {glob}\");\n        }\n        assert_eq!(allows.len(), ALLOW_GLOBS.len());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn exclude_globs_skip_unmeasured_proc_macro_crate() {\n        assert!(\n            EXCLUDE_GLOBS.contains(&\"crates/ars-derive/**\"),\n            \"ars-derive is unmeasured by llvm-cov and must be excluded\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn format_from_ci_flag_maps_github_actions_true() {\n        assert_eq!(format_from_ci_flag(Some(\"true\")), Format::Github);\n        assert_eq!(format_from_ci_flag(Some(\"false\")), Format::Human);\n        assert_eq!(format_from_ci_flag(None), Format::Human);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn format_as_arg_round_trips() {\n        assert_eq!(Format::Human.as_arg(), \"human\");\n        assert_eq!(Format::Github.as_arg(), \"github\");\n    }\n}\n"
}

101	/// Pipeline profile selecting which step list to run when no positional steps
102	/// are passed.
103	///
104	/// - [`Profile::Full`] mirrors the GitHub Actions CI job exactly (20 gates,
105	///   ~25 minutes locally) and is the default for backward compatibility with
106	///   scripts that call `cargo xci` directly.
107	/// - [`Profile::Fast`] runs the curated [`FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`] subset
108	///   (~5 minutes) intended as the routine pre-push gate. It drops the coverage
109	///   recompile, the wasm browser tests, the release-profile checks, and the
110	///   five `feature-matrix-*` groups — all of which are CI-only concerns that
111	///   re-test what `Unit` / `Integration` / `Adapter` already covered.
112	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default, clap::ValueEnum)]
113	#[value(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
114	pub enum Profile {
115	    /// Full CI parity (default). Runs every step in [`PIPELINE_ORDER`].
116	    #[default]
117	    Full,
118	
119	    /// Fast pre-push gate. Runs every step in [`FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`].
120	    Fast,
121	}
122

129	    Step::Fmt,
130	    Step::Clippy,

133	    Step::DomBrowser,
134	    Step::Release,
135	    Step::Integration,

137	    Step::AdapterParity,
138	    Step::Coverage,

142	    Step::ErrorVariantCoverage,
143	    Step::FeatureMatrixCore,
144	    Step::FeatureMatrixI18n,
145	    Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems,
146	    Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos,
147	    Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus,

154	/// Covers cross-crate / workspace-level regressions and the structural lints

157	/// groups — those run in CI and rarely change the outcome of a local pre-push
158	/// gate when `Unit`, `Integration`, `Adapter`, and `--all-features check`
159	/// have already passed.
160	///
161	/// Power users who touched wasm or feature-gated code can opt the skipped

163	/// positional steps still override the profile.

178	/// Errors from CI operations.
179	#[derive(Debug)]
180	pub enum Error {
181	    /// A cargo subprocess exited with a non-zero status.
182	    StepFailed {
183	        /// The step that failed.
184	        step: Step,
185	
186	        /// The command that was run (for display).
187	        command: String,
188	
189	        /// Process exit code, if available.
190	        code: Option<i32>,
191	    },
192	
193	    /// A required tool is not installed.
194	    MissingTool {
195	        /// Human-readable tool name.
196	        tool: String,
197	
198	        /// How to install it.
199	        install_hint: String,
200	    },
201	
202	    /// IO error spawning a subprocess.
203	    Io(io::Error),
204	
205	    /// Coverage threshold check failed.
206	    Coverage(coverage::Error),
207	
208	    /// Repository lint check failed.
209	    Lint(lint::Error),
210	
211	    /// Spec corpus check failed (e.g. `compile-snippets` reported a Rust
212	    /// syntax error in a fenced code block).
213	    Spec(manifest::Error),
214	}
215	
216	impl Display for Error {
217	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
218	        match self {
219	            Self::StepFailed {
220	                step,
221	                command,
222	                code,
223	            } => {
224	                write!(f, "{} failed", step_name(*step))?;
225	
226	                if let Some(code) = code {
227	                    write!(f, " (exit code {code})")?;
228	                }
229	
230	                write!(f, ": {command}")
231	            }
232	
233	            Self::MissingTool { tool, install_hint } => {
234	                write!(
235	                    f,
236	                    "missing required tool: {tool}\n  install: {install_hint}"
237	                )
238	            }
239	
240	            Self::Io(e) => write!(f, "IO error: {e}"),
241	
242	            Self::Coverage(e) => write!(f, "{e}"),
243	
244	            Self::Lint(e) => write!(f, "{e}"),
245	
246	            Self::Spec(e) => write!(f, "{e}"),
247	        }
248	    }
249	}
250	
251	impl std::error::Error for Error {}
252	
253	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
254	// Public entry point
255	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
256	
257	/// Run the specified CI steps.
258	///
259	/// When `steps` is empty, the step list is taken from the `profile`'s default
260	/// pipeline ([`PIPELINE_ORDER`] for [`Profile::Full`], [`FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER`]
261	/// for [`Profile::Fast`]). When `steps` is non-empty, the positional list
262	/// runs as-is and `profile` is ignored — this preserves the existing
263	/// `cargo xci fmt check` ad-hoc selection semantics.
264	///
265	/// Execution is sequential and fail-fast: the first step that fails stops the
266	/// pipeline and returns its error.
```

Now make `step_name_covers_all_variants` exhaustive and self-maintaining via clap's `value_variants()`, and add a test pinning `Crap` directly after `Coverage`:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01Piw2ZzXGxZFGxbWntrL5sF
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui/xtask/src/ci/mod.rs",
  "old_string": "    /// Every `CiStep` variant has a non-empty name.\n    #[test]\n    fn step_name_covers_all_variants() {\n        let all = [\n            Step::Fmt,\n            Step::Check,\n            Step::Clippy,\n            Step::Unit,\n            Step::I18nBrowser,\n            Step::DomBrowser,\n            Step::Release,\n            Step::Integration,\n            Step::Adapter,\n            Step::AdapterParity,\n            Step::Coverage,\n            Step::SnapshotCount,\n            Step::ErrorVariantCoverage,\n            Step::FeatureMatrix,\n            Step::FeatureMatrixCore,\n            Step::FeatureMatrixI18n,\n            Step::FeatureMatrixSubsystems,\n            Step::FeatureMatrixLeptos,\n            Step::FeatureMatrixDioxus,\n            Step::MutualExclusion,\n        ];\n\n        for step in all {\n            assert!(!step_name(step).is_empty(), \"{step:?} has empty name\");\n        }\n    }",
  "new_string": "    /// Every `Step` variant has a non-empty name. Iterates the clap-derived\n    /// variant list so the test stays exhaustive as variants are added or\n    /// removed without a hand-maintained mirror.\n    #[test]\n    fn step_name_covers_all_variants() {\n        use clap::ValueEnum as _;\n\n        for step in Step::value_variants() {\n            assert!(!step_name(*step).is_empty(), \"{step:?} has empty name\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn crap_runs_immediately_after_coverage() {\n        // The CRAP gate consumes the merged `lcov.info` that the coverage step\n        // writes, so it must sit directly after `Coverage` in the full pipeline.\n        let coverage = PIPELINE_ORDER\n            .iter()\n            .position(|&step| step == Step::Coverage)\n            .expect(\"PIPELINE_ORDER contains Coverage\");\n\n        let crap = PIPELINE_ORDER\n            .iter()\n            .position(|&step| step == Step::Crap)\n            .expect(\"PIPELINE_ORDER contains Crap\");\n\n        assert_eq!(\n            crap,\n            coverage + 1,\n            \"Crap must run immediately after Coverage\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn check_step_is_fully_removed() {\n        // `check` is redundant with `clippy --all-targets --all-features` and was\n        // removed from both pipelines. Guard against a reflexive re-add.\n        use clap::ValueEnum as _;\n\n        assert!(\n            Step::value_variants()\n                .iter()\n                .all(|step| step_name(*step) != \"check\"),\n            \"the redundant `check` step must not be reintroduced\"\n        );\n    }"
}
```

{

  "old_string": "        /// Override the workspace default `--features` string for this package.\n        #[arg(long)]\n        features: Option<String>,\n    },\n}",
  "new_string": "        /// Override the workspace default `--features` string for this package.\n        #[arg(long)]\n        features: Option<String>,\n    },\n\n    /// Run the cargo-crap CRAP gate locally (alias: `cargo xcrap`).\n    ///\n    /// Reads an existing lcov report (generate one with `cargo xci coverage`)\n    /// and reports functions whose complexity-vs-coverage CRAP score exceeds\n    /// the threshold. Unlike the `cargo xci crap` CI step, this never fails the\n    /// process on a breach — it is a readable local report.\n    Crap {\n        /// Path to the lcov coverage report.\n        #[arg(long, default_value = \"lcov.info\")]\n        lcov: PathBuf,\n\n        /// CRAP score threshold (defaults to the gate's pinned threshold).\n        #[arg(long)]\n        threshold: Option<u32>,\n    },\n}"
}
```

}

```
82	
83	# Check all crates against spec-defined thresholds (run after a *merged* lcov)
84	cargo xtask coverage check-all --file lcov.info
85	```
86	
87	The `--text` flag produces line-by-line annotated source showing hit counts and `^0` markers on uncovered branches — use this to identify gaps after writing tests. Uncovered no-op callback closures in tests (e.g., `|_| {}`) are expected and not a gap.
88	
89	The local recipe above excludes the adapter and adapter-test-harness crates because their `target_arch = "wasm32"` / `feature = "web"` code paths can't be measured via host-target `cargo llvm-cov`. **CI runs an additional wasm-coverage pipeline on nightly** (`cargo xtask ci coverage`) that uses `wasm-bindgen-test`'s experimental coverage recipe with LLVM 22 / `clang-22` to emit lcov for `ars-leptos` (csr), `ars-dioxus` (web), `ars-dom` (web), `ars-i18n` (web-intl), and the adapter test harnesses, then merges with the native lcov via `cargo xtask coverage merge`. The per-crate thresholds in `xtask::coverage::default_thresholds()` are enforced against the merged file — including adapter targets (`ars-leptos` 74/55, `ars-dioxus` 77/70, `ars-test-harness-leptos` 60/0, `ars-test-harness-dioxus` 60/0). `ars-derive` (proc-macro, runs in compiler) is exercised via `crates/ars-core/tests/derive_contract.rs` and is unmeasured. `xtask` has its own enforced threshold (30/35) and is measured. See `spec/testing/14-ci.md` §2 for the full pipeline.
90	
91	### Mutation Testing
92	
93	Coverage tells you which lines run. **Mutation testing tells you whether the tests would notice if those lines silently misbehaved.** The workspace uses [`cargo-mutants`](https://mutants.rs) for this — a `cargo` extension binary, not a workspace dependency.

See `spec/testing/14-ci.md` §2 for the full pipeline.\n\n### Mutation Testing",
  "new_string": "The local recipe above excludes the adapter and adapter-test-harness crates because their `target_arch = \"wasm32\"` / `feature = \"web\"` code paths can't be measured via host-target `cargo llvm-cov`. **CI runs an additional wasm-coverage pipeline on nightly** (`cargo xtask ci coverage`) that uses `wasm-bindgen-test`'s experimental coverage recipe with LLVM 22 / `clang-22` to emit lcov for `ars-leptos` (csr), `ars-dioxus` (web), `ars-dom` (web), `ars-i18n` (web-intl), and the adapter test harnesses, then merges with the native lcov via `cargo xtask coverage merge`. The per-crate thresholds in `xtask::coverage::default_thresholds()` are enforced against the merged file — including adapter targets (`ars-leptos` 74/55, `ars-dioxus` 77/70, `ars-test-harness-leptos` 60/0, `ars-test-harness-dioxus` 60/0). `ars-derive` (proc-macro, runs in compiler) is exercised via `crates/ars-core/tests/derive_contract.rs` and is unmeasured. `xtask` has its own enforced threshold (30/35) and is measured. See `spec/testing/14-ci.md` §2 for the full pipeline.\n\n### CRAP Gate (complexity vs. coverage)\n\nCoverage tells you which lines run; mutation testing tells you whether tests would notice misbehaviour. The **CRAP gate** ([`cargo-crap`](https://crates.io/crates/cargo-crap)) tells you which functions are *risky to change* — high cyclomatic complexity combined with low coverage. The CRAP score is `complexity² · (1 − coverage)³ + complexity`: well-covered code stays low even when complex, so the gate mainly surfaces complex-and-undertested functions.\n\n- **Blessed entrypoints:** `cargo xcrap` (local, human-readable report, never fails) and `cargo xtask ci crap` (the CI gate). The gate is the last step of the full `cargo xci` pipeline, running immediately after `coverage` and reusing its merged `lcov.info` (no recompile). It is **not** in the fast profile (which skips coverage).\n- **Policy:** `cargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info --fail-above --threshold 30` — fails CI if any function exceeds the CRAP threshold. 30 is cargo-crap's built-in default and the canonical Crap4J cutoff.\n- **Pinned version:** cargo-crap is pinned to an exact version in `xtask::crap::CARGO_CRAP_VERSION` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (same convention as cargo-mutants). To bump, update both and re-run a calibration pass. Install locally with `cargo install cargo-crap --locked --version <version>` (or `cargo binstall cargo-crap`).\n- **Escape hatches:** the exclude- and allow-glob lists live in `xtask/src/crap.rs` (`EXCLUDE_GLOBS`, `ALLOW_GLOBS`), each entry justified by a comment. `crates/ars-derive/**` is excluded because llvm-cov never instruments proc-macro crates (they would score 0% coverage and explode CRAP). **Prefer adding tests over adding an allow entry** — covering a function lowers its CRAP score directly. Only adjust `CRAP_THRESHOLD` as a last resort.\n\n### Mutation Testing"
}

> AGENT

Now the decisive insight surfaces. The coverage field is a 0–100 percentage (so `cov=100%` shows as my mis-scaled "10000%"). The 126 over-threshold measured functions split cleanly:

- **80 at 0% coverage** — mostly xtask tooling internals (`main` CC 61, `run_step`, `execute`) that aren't unit-tested, plus `ars-i18n` web-intl-backend functions my native run doesn't exercise.
- **46 at ≥80% coverage but high CC** — e.g. `KeyboardKey::from_key_str` (CC **287**, 100% covered), `CssProperty::fmt` (CC 161, covered). At 100% coverage `CRAP == cyclomatic complexity`, so **any function with CC > 30 fails the gate no matter how well-tested it is.** These are big exhaustive `match` statements — nothing to "fix."

Let me quantify the CI-stable problem (well-covered-but-complex in shipped library crates) and what threshold would clear it:

> AGENT

Confirmed the noise: `part_attrs` is real shipped API (per-component `match` over `Part`), while the `assert_*_send_result_invariants` are **test helpers** in `tests/proptest_state_machines/` that `--workspace` wrongly walked. 

This calibration decisively changes the picture, so I need to bring it back to you before locking the gate. Here's what the fresh data shows:

**The problem with absolute "fail-above 30":** At 100% coverage, `CRAP == cyclomatic complexity`. So **34 fully-tested library functions** (e.g. `KeyboardKey::from_key_str` CC **287**, `CssProperty::fmt` CC 161 — 23 in components, 6 in core, 5 in a11y) exceed threshold 30 and **cannot be fixed by adding tests** — they're big exhaustive `match` statements. No clean threshold separates them (the max is CC 287; lib functions over 30/50/75/100 = 65/34/22/12). Plus the `--workspace` walk pulls in `tests/` helpers and generated `part_attrs` impls as noise.

So hard-fail-30 would require allow-listing ~34+ legitimately-complex tested functions and refactoring big matches purely to satisfy the metric. That's the scenario cargo-crap's author flags for mature codebases.

If those functions are too complex and fully tested already, having them split into smalled functions would help?

> AGENT

Good question — and the answer has an important nuance.

**Mechanically: yes.** cargo-crap scores *per function*, and cyclomatic complexity is per-function. If you split a CC=287 function into ten CC≈29 helpers, each helper scores under 30 and the gate passes. So decomposition directly lowers CRAP.

**But whether it's worth doing depends on the function's shape**, and this is where cyclomatic complexity is a known-weak signal:

- **Wide-but-flat** functions — a big exhaustive `match` like `KeyboardKey::from_key_str` (287 arms mapping key-strings → enum) or `CssProperty::fmt`. CC counts every arm as a branch, so the number is huge, but the function has no nesting, no interacting conditions, is trivially correct, and the compiler checks exhaustiveness. Splitting it into "letters / digits / function-keys" sub-matches purely to satisfy the metric is **metric-gaming** — it doesn't reduce real risk, and can scatter a cohesive lookup. (If anything, the genuine improvement for these is a data table / `phf` map, not arbitrary function-splitting.)
- **Tangled** functions — deep nesting, many interacting branches, shared mutable state. Here high CC *does* signal risk, and splitting is a real improvement worth doing.

So "split them all" would force low-value churn on the flat matches. The right move is to look at the 34 and separate the two kinds: refactor the genuinely tangled ones, and for the flat exhaustive matches either accept them (baseline/allow) or convert to data tables where that's actually cleaner.

This also reframes the gate decision: if you're willing to refactor the tangled ones and baseline/allow the flat ones, hard-fail becomes workable — it's not all-or-nothing.

> AGENT

Pulling the actual 34 makes the situation much clearer — they fall into exactly two families:

**Family A — flat enum↔string lookups (10 functions, the highest CC):**
`KeyboardKey::from_key_str` (287) / `as_w3c_str` (286), `CssProperty::fmt` (161), `HtmlAttr::static_name` (145), `HtmlEvent::fmt` (94), `AriaRole`/`AriaAttribute`/`AriaAttr` mappings (50–87). These are pure exhaustive lookup tables. Splitting them into sub-matches is metric-gaming; the only "real" alternative is a data table / `phf` map, and even that's marginal. Not worth refactoring for the metric.

**Family B — `Machine::transition` (24 functions, CC 31–77):** `table`, `tree_view`, `hover_card`, `combobox`, `listbox`, `select`, `tabs`, `tooltip`, `slider`, `number_input`… **This is the library's central design pattern.** Every stateful component's transition is a wide `match (state, event)` — intrinsically CC 30–77, and they're all 90–100% covered.

That second group is the real headline: **the gate is firing on the architecture itself.** A state-machine framework will always have ~one CC>30 `transition` per stateful component, and they're exactly the code you've invested most in testing.

So to your question directly:

- **Splitting the Family A lookups** → metric-gaming, no real risk reduction. Don't.
- **Splitting the 24 `Machine::transition` functions** → mechanically it works (extract per-event `fn on_open`/`fn on_close` helpers, each CC<30), and for a *few* it might read better. But doing it across 24 components is a large, high-churn refactor of the most critical, already-100%-covered code in the codebase, purely to satisfy a complexity metric. The cyclomatic number on a `(state × event)` dispatch match is a poor risk signal — coverage already tells you these are exercised. I'd advise against it as a blanket remedy.

This is why I think **baseline/regression mode is the natural fit**: it accepts the 34 existing well-tested complex functions as the baseline, then fails only when CRAP gets *worse* — which still catches the actual risk (a new untested arm added to a `transition`, or a brand-new complex+undertested function from AI-assisted edits), without forcing you to either refactor your core pattern or maintain a 34-entry allow-list.

The honest alternative view: if a complexity gate fundamentally fights a state-machine architecture, you might decide CRAP belongs in **advisory** mode (surfaces numbers, never blocks) rather than as a hard gate at all.

Want me to go with baseline/regression (my recommendation), keep it advisory-only, or would you like to look at a couple of the `transition` functions first to judge the split question for yourself?

> DEVELOPER

We will still have a few more agnostic components to add, with more state machines like that, won't it be an issue?

> AGENT

That's the sharpest question yet — and it gets to whether *any* hard gate fits this architecture long-term. Let me reason it through, then verify the one fact the answer hinges on rather than guess.

**The structural math, projected forward:** every new stateful component adds one `Machine::transition` with CC ~30–77, and you test them to ~95–100%. At high coverage, `CRAP ≈ CC`. So:
- No absolute threshold survives. To let a well-tested CC=77 transition pass you'd need threshold ≥ 78 — but then an *undertested* CC=10 function (CRAP 110) is the only thing that fails, and undertested CC=8 (CRAP 72) sails through. The Family-A lookups (CC 287) push the required threshold to absurd. Absolute fail-above is structurally wrong here, and stays wrong for every component you add.

So the real question for "does it scale" is entirely about **baseline mode's behavior on *new* functions** — does `--fail-regression` fail when a brand-new component introduces a new CC>30 transition? If yes, baseline is just deferred pain. If new functions are tolerated, baseline scales but is weaker. I won't guess cargo-crap's semantics — let me test it:

> AGENT

Empirically settled — here are the exact `--fail-regression` semantics:

| Scenario | Result |
|---|---|
| **A.** New function (CC 77, well-covered) absent from baseline | **exit 0** — counted "new", not "regressed" |
| **B.** Empty baseline → all 9064 functions "new" | **exit 0** |
| **C.** Existing function's CRAP rose vs baseline | **exit 1** — "2 regressed" |

So `--fail-regression` fails **only when an already-tracked function gets worse.** New functions are always tolerated.

**That directly answers your concern: no, future state machines are not an issue under baseline mode.** Each new component's `transition` (CC 30–77, well-tested) is a *new* function → passes automatically, with no per-component tax and no baseline edit required.

And it reveals a clean division of labor with gates you already have:

- **Coverage gate** (existing per-crate llvm-cov thresholds) → ensures *new* code is tested. So a new component shipped with an undertested transition is caught here, not by CRAP.
- **CRAP regression gate** (proposed) → ensures *existing* code doesn't rot: if someone adds three untested arms to `table::Machine::transition`, its CRAP rises → CI fails. This is the thing nothing else currently catches.

Together they cover "new code is tested" + "shipped code doesn't decay," with no double-counting and no fight with the state-machine architecture.

That makes **baseline/regression the clear fit.** Two operational notes so it's not flaky:
- The baseline (`.crap-baseline.json`, committed) must be generated from CI's **real merged** lcov (native+wasm), not my local native proxy — so the first generation happens in CI.
- Coverage % jitters slightly run-to-run; cargo-crap's `--epsilon` (default 0.01) absorbs tiny deltas. When you *intentionally* raise a function's complexity, you regenerate and commit the baseline in that PR — a deliberate "I accept this" checkpoint.

The only real alternative remains **advisory** (annotations, never blocks) if you'd rather not maintain a baseline file at all.

My recommendation: go with **baseline/regression**. Want me to implement that (rework the gate to `--baseline .crap-baseline.json --fail-regression`, add a `cargo xcrap --update-baseline` path, and generate the initial baseline in the coverage job), or would you prefer advisory-only?

`--min 30` is exactly the knob. Combined results:

| Gate config | Sub-threshold regression (crap 3→5) | Above-threshold regression (→77) |
|---|---|---|
| `--fail-regression` alone | ❌ fails (too noisy) | ❌ fails |
| `--fail-regression --min 30` | ✅ **exit 0** (ignored) | ❌ fails (caught) |

So the final gate is `--baseline --fail-regression --min 30`: tolerates new functions, ignores routine churn on simple functions, and fails only when an **already-complex (≥30) function gets worse**. That's low-noise, scales to future state machines, and complements the coverage gate. This also lets me drop the github-annotation idea (it would spam ~248 `::warning`s for all current ≥30 functions); the failure log table is clear enough.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui/xtask/src/crap.rs",
  "content": "//! CRAP-metric gate via [`cargo-crap`](https://crates.io/crates/cargo-crap).\n//!\n//! Powers `cargo xcrap` and the `crap` CI step. The CRAP score\n//! (`complexity² · (1 − coverage)³ + complexity`) flags functions that are both\n//! cyclomatically complex and poorly covered.\n//!\n//! ## Why regression mode, not an absolute threshold\n//!\n//! At 100% coverage `CRAP == cyclomatic complexity`, so an absolute\n//! `--fail-above` threshold rejects every large `match` regardless of how well\n//! it is tested. This workspace is built on state machines: each stateful\n//! component has a `Machine::transition` that is intrinsically a wide\n//! `match (state, event)` with complexity 30–80, all exhaustively tested. An\n//! absolute gate would fight that architecture and fail more components as the\n//! catalog grows.\n//!\n//! Instead the gate runs in **regression mode**: it compares against a committed\n//! [`BASELINE_PATH`] and fails only when an *already-complex* function\n//! (`--min` [`CRAP_THRESHOLD`]) gets *worse* than the baseline — more complexity\n//! or less coverage. Brand-new functions are tolerated (the per-crate coverage\n//! thresholds already ensure new code is tested); routine edits to simple\n//! functions below the threshold are ignored. The net effect: the coverage gate\n//! guarantees new code is tested, and this gate guarantees shipped code does not\n//! rot. The gate reuses the merged `lcov.info` the coverage step produces, so it\n//! never recompiles.\n\nuse std::{\n    fmt::{self, Display},\n    io,\n    path::{Path, PathBuf},\n    process::{self, Stdio},\n};\n\n/// Pinned `cargo-crap` version — keep in sync with `.github/workflows/ci.yml`\n/// and the CLAUDE.md note. Pinning is deliberate: cargo-crap's scoring can\n/// change between releases, and an unpinned install would silently shift the\n/// gate without a code change.\npub const CARGO_CRAP_VERSION: &str = \"0.2.2\";\n\n/// CRAP score at or above which a function is tracked by the gate (forwarded as\n/// `--min`) and marked \"crappy\" in reports (`--threshold`). Matches cargo-crap's\n/// built-in default and the canonical Crap4J cutoff. Functions below this are\n/// ignored by the regression gate, so routine edits to simple code never fail\n/// CI.\npub const CRAP_THRESHOLD: u32 = 30;\n\n/// Repo-root path of the committed CRAP baseline. The gate fails when a tracked\n/// function regresses against this file; regenerate it with\n/// `cargo xcrap --update-baseline` (from CI's merged `lcov.info`) when a\n/// deliberate complexity increase lands.\npub const BASELINE_PATH: &str = \".crap-baseline.json\";\n\n/// Functions suppressed from the report and baseline (`--allow`). Every entry\n/// must carry a justification. Under `--workspace`, cargo-crap's `--exclude`\n/// (relative to `--path`) is a no-op; path suppression must go through `--allow`,\n/// which is honored.\nconst ALLOW_GLOBS: &[&str] = &[\n    // `ars-derive` is a proc-macro crate: its code runs inside the compiler, so\n    // `cargo-llvm-cov` never instruments it. Every function would otherwise be\n    // scored at 0% coverage and dominate the baseline with noise. It is\n    // exercised via `crates/ars-core/tests/derive_contract.rs`.\n    \"crates/ars-derive/**\",\n];\n\n/// What the cargo-crap invocation should do.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum Action {\n    /// CI gate: fail if any tracked (`--min` [`CRAP_THRESHOLD`]) function's CRAP\n    /// score regressed against the baseline.\n    Gate,\n\n    /// Local human-readable report. Never fails the process.\n    Report,\n\n    /// (Re)generate the baseline JSON from the current lcov.\n    UpdateBaseline,\n}\n\n/// Options for a cargo-crap invocation.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct Options {\n    /// What to do.\n    pub action: Action,\n\n    /// Path to the lcov coverage report (typically `lcov.info`).\n    pub lcov: PathBuf,\n\n    /// Path to the baseline JSON (typically [`BASELINE_PATH`]).\n    pub baseline: PathBuf,\n\n    /// CRAP score cutoff forwarded as `--min` (gate) or `--threshold` (report).\n    pub threshold: u32,\n}\n\n/// Errors returned by the CRAP gate.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub enum Error {\n    /// `cargo-crap` is not installed or not on `PATH`.\n    MissingTool {\n        /// Human-readable tool name.\n        tool: String,\n\n        /// Suggested install command.\n        install_hint: String,\n    },\n\n    /// The lcov coverage report does not exist.\n    MissingLcov {\n        /// The path that was expected to hold the report.\n        path: PathBuf,\n    },\n\n    /// The subprocess exited unsuccessfully (a CRAP regression, or a cargo-crap\n    /// error).\n    CommandFailed {\n        /// Exit code, if available.\n        code: Option<i32>,\n    },\n\n    /// IO error while spawning the subprocess.\n    Io(io::Error),\n}\n\nimpl Display for Error {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            Self::MissingTool { tool, install_hint } => {\n                write!(\n                    f,\n                    \"missing required tool: {tool}\\n  install: {install_hint}\"\n                )\n            }\n\n            Self::MissingLcov { path } => {\n                write!(\n                    f,\n                    \"coverage report not found: {}\\n  run `cargo xci coverage` first to generate it\",\n                    path.display()\n                )\n            }\n\n            Self::CommandFailed { code } => {\n                write!(f, \"cargo crap reported a CRAP regression\")?;\n\n                if let Some(code) = code {\n                    write!(f, \" (exit {code})\")?;\n                }\n\n                Ok(())\n            }\n\n            Self::Io(err) => err.fmt(f),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl From<io::Error> for Error {\n    fn from(value: io::Error) -> Self {\n        Self::Io(value)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Builds the `cargo crap …` argument vector for `options`.\n///\n/// The leading `\"crap\"` is the subcommand name cargo injects when a binary is\n/// invoked as `cargo crap`; cargo-crap tolerates it. Split out as a pure\n/// function so the argument surface is unit-testable.\nfn build_args(options: &Options) -> Vec<String> {\n    let mut args = vec![\n        \"crap\".to_owned(),\n        \"--workspace\".to_owned(),\n        \"--lcov\".to_owned(),\n        options.lcov.display().to_string(),\n    ];\n\n    match options.action {\n        Action::Gate => {\n            // Track only functions at/above the threshold (`--min`) and fail\n            // when one of them regressed against the baseline. New functions and\n            // sub-threshold churn are tolerated.\n            args.push(\"--baseline\".to_owned());\n            args.push(options.baseline.display().to_string());\n            args.push(\"--fail-regression\".to_owned());\n            args.push(\"--min\".to_owned());\n            args.push(options.threshold.to_string());\n        }\n\n        Action::Report => {\n            args.push(\"--threshold\".to_owned());\n            args.push(options.threshold.to_string());\n        }\n\n        Action::UpdateBaseline => {\n            // A complete baseline (no `--min`) is required: a function that\n            // crosses from below the threshold to above it must be present in\n            // the baseline at its old score, or the increase reads as \"new\" and\n            // escapes the gate.\n            args.push(\"--format\".to_owned());\n            args.push(\"json\".to_owned());\n            args.push(\"--output\".to_owned());\n            args.push(options.baseline.display().to_string());\n        }\n    }\n\n    for glob in ALLOW_GLOBS {\n        args.push(\"--allow\".to_owned());\n        args.push((*glob).to_owned());\n    }\n\n    args\n}\n\n/// Runs cargo-crap for `options`.\n///\n/// In [`Action::Gate`] mode, a missing baseline is bootstrapped: the baseline is\n/// generated from the current lcov and the gate passes with a notice, so the\n/// first run on a repo without a committed baseline does not fail spuriously.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// Returns [`Error::MissingTool`] when `cargo-crap` is unavailable,\n/// [`Error::MissingLcov`] when the coverage report is absent,\n/// [`Error::CommandFailed`] when the subprocess exits non-zero (a CRAP\n/// regression), or [`Error::Io`] on spawn failures.\npub fn run(options: &Options) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    preflight_cargo_crap()?;\n    preflight_lcov(&options.lcov)?;\n\n    if options.action == Action::Gate && !options.baseline.exists() {\n        eprintln!(\n            \"note: baseline {} not found; generating it from {} (gate is advisory until committed)\",\n            options.baseline.display(),\n            options.lcov.display()\n        );\n\n        let bootstrap = Options {\n            action: Action::UpdateBaseline,\n            ..options.clone()\n        };\n\n        run_cargo_crap(&bootstrap)?;\n\n        eprintln!(\n            \"note: wrote {} — commit it to activate the regression gate\",\n            options.baseline.display()\n        );\n\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n\n    run_cargo_crap(options)\n}\n\nfn run_cargo_crap(options: &Options) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    let args = build_args(options);\n\n    eprintln!(\"  > cargo {}\", args.join(\" \"));\n\n    let status = process::Command::new(\"cargo\")\n        .args(&args)\n        .stdin(Stdio::inherit())\n        .stdout(Stdio::inherit())\n        .stderr(Stdio::inherit())\n        .status()?;\n\n    if status.success() {\n        Ok(())\n    } else {\n        Err(Error::CommandFailed {\n            code: status.code(),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\nfn preflight_cargo_crap() -> Result<(), Error> {\n    let output = process::Command::new(\"cargo\")\n        .args([\"crap\", \"--version\"])\n        .output()?;\n\n    if output.status.success() {\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n\n    Err(Error::MissingTool {\n        tool: \"cargo-crap\".into(),\n        install_hint: format!(\"cargo install cargo-crap --locked --version {CARGO_CRAP_VERSION}\"),\n    })\n}\n\nfn preflight_lcov(lcov: &Path) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    if lcov.exists() {\n        Ok(())\n    } else {\n        Err(Error::MissingLcov {\n            path: lcov.to_path_buf(),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    fn options(action: Action) -> Options {\n        Options {\n            action,\n            lcov: PathBuf::from(\"lcov.info\"),\n            baseline: PathBuf::from(BASELINE_PATH),\n            threshold: CRAP_THRESHOLD,\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn values_after<'a>(args: &'a [String], flag: &str) -> Vec<&'a str> {\n        args.windows(2)\n            .filter(|pair| pair[0] == flag)\n            .map(|pair| pair[1].as_str())\n            .collect()\n    }\n\n    fn has_flag(args: &[String], flag: &str) -> bool {\n        args.iter().any(|arg| arg == flag)\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn cargo_crap_version_matches_ci_pin() {\n        assert_eq!(CARGO_CRAP_VERSION, \"0.2.2\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn threshold_is_thirty() {\n        assert_eq!(CRAP_THRESHOLD, 30);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn all_actions_start_with_crap_workspace_and_lcov() {\n        for action in [Action::Gate, Action::Report, Action::UpdateBaseline] {\n            let args = build_args(&options(action));\n\n            assert_eq!(args[0], \"crap\");\n            assert!(has_flag(&args, \"--workspace\"));\n            assert_eq!(values_after(&args, \"--lcov\"), vec![\"lcov.info\"]);\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn gate_uses_baseline_regression_and_min() {\n        let args = build_args(&options(Action::Gate));\n\n        assert_eq!(values_after(&args, \"--baseline\"), vec![BASELINE_PATH]);\n        assert!(has_flag(&args, \"--fail-regression\"));\n        assert_eq!(values_after(&args, \"--min\"), vec![\"30\"]);\n        // The gate must never use the absolute fail-above mode.\n        assert!(!has_flag(&args, \"--fail-above\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn report_uses_threshold_and_never_fails() {\n        let args = build_args(&options(Action::Report));\n\n        assert_eq!(values_after(&args, \"--threshold\"), vec![\"30\"]);\n        assert!(!has_flag(&args, \"--fail-regression\"));\n        assert!(!has_flag(&args, \"--fail-above\"));\n        assert!(!has_flag(&args, \"--baseline\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn update_baseline_writes_complete_json_without_min() {\n        let args = build_args(&options(Action::UpdateBaseline));\n\n        assert_eq!(values_after(&args, \"--format\"), vec![\"json\"]);\n        assert_eq!(values_after(&args, \"--output\"), vec![BASELINE_PATH]);\n        assert!(!has_flag(&args, \"--fail-regression\"));\n        // No `--min`: the baseline must record every function, including\n        // sub-threshold ones that could later cross the threshold.\n        assert!(!has_flag(&args, \"--min\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn every_action_forwards_allow_globs() {\n        for action in [Action::Gate, Action::Report, Action::UpdateBaseline] {\n            let allows = values_after(&build_args(&options(action)), \"--allow\");\n\n            for glob in ALLOW_GLOBS {\n                assert!(allows.contains(glob), \"{action:?} missing --allow {glob}\");\n            }\n            assert_eq!(allows.len(), ALLOW_GLOBS.len());\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn allow_globs_suppress_unmeasured_proc_macro_crate() {\n        assert!(\n            ALLOW_GLOBS.contains(&\"crates/ars-derive/**\"),\n            \"ars-derive is unmeasured by llvm-cov and must be suppressed\"\n        );\n    }\n}\n"
}

Unlike the `cargo xci crap` CI step, this never fails the\n    /// process on a breach — it is a readable local report.\n    Crap {\n        /// Path to the lcov coverage report.\n        #[arg(long, default_value = \"lcov.info\")]\n        lcov: PathBuf,\n\n        /// CRAP score threshold (defaults to the gate's pinned threshold).\n        #[arg(long)]\n        threshold: Option<u32>,\n    },\n}",

Unlike the `cargo xci crap` CI gate, this never fails the\n    /// process — it is a readable local report.\n    ///\n    /// Pass `--update-baseline` to (re)generate the committed regression\n    /// baseline from the lcov instead, e.g. after a deliberate complexity\n    /// increase or to refresh it from CI's merged `lcov.info`.\n    Crap {\n        /// Path to the lcov coverage report.\n        #[arg(long, default_value = \"lcov.info\")]\n        lcov: PathBuf,\n\n        /// CRAP score threshold (defaults to the gate's pinned threshold).\n        #[arg(long)]\n        threshold: Option<u32>,\n\n        /// Regenerate the committed baseline instead of printing a report.\n        #[arg(long)]\n        update_baseline: bool,\n    },\n}"

  "old_string": "### CRAP Gate (complexity vs. coverage)\n\nCoverage tells you which lines run; mutation testing tells you whether tests would notice misbehaviour. The **CRAP gate** ([`cargo-crap`](https://crates.io/crates/cargo-crap)) tells you which functions are _risky to change_ — high cyclomatic complexity combined with low coverage. The CRAP score is `complexity² · (1 − coverage)³ + complexity`: well-covered code stays low even when complex, so the gate mainly surfaces complex-and-undertested functions.\n\n- **Blessed entrypoints:** `cargo xcrap` (local, human-readable report, never fails) and `cargo xtask ci crap` (the CI gate). The gate is the last step of the full `cargo xci` pipeline, running immediately after `coverage` and reusing its merged `lcov.info` (no recompile). It is **not** in the fast profile (which skips coverage).\n- **Policy:** `cargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info --fail-above --threshold 30` — fails CI if any function exceeds the CRAP threshold. 30 is cargo-crap's built-in default and the canonical Crap4J cutoff.\n- **Pinned version:** cargo-crap is pinned to an exact version in `xtask::crap::CARGO_CRAP_VERSION` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (same convention as cargo-mutants). To bump, update both and re-run a calibration pass. Install locally with `cargo install cargo-crap --locked --version <version>` (or `cargo binstall cargo-crap`).\n- **Escape hatches:** the exclude- and allow-glob lists live in `xtask/src/crap.rs` (`EXCLUDE_GLOBS`, `ALLOW_GLOBS`), each entry justified by a comment. `crates/ars-derive/**` is excluded because llvm-cov never instruments proc-macro crates (they would score 0% coverage and explode CRAP). **Prefer adding tests over adding an allow entry** — covering a function lowers its CRAP score directly. Only adjust `CRAP_THRESHOLD` as a last resort.",
  "new_string": "### CRAP Gate (complexity vs. coverage)\n\nCoverage tells you which lines run; mutation testing tells you whether tests would notice misbehaviour. The **CRAP gate** ([`cargo-crap`](https://crates.io/crates/cargo-crap)) tells you which functions are _risky to change_ — high cyclomatic complexity combined with low coverage. The CRAP score is `complexity² · (1 − coverage)³ + complexity`.\n\n**Why regression mode, not an absolute threshold.** At 100% coverage `CRAP == cyclomatic complexity`, so an absolute `--fail-above` threshold rejects every large `match` no matter how well it is tested. This workspace is built on state machines: each stateful component's `Machine::transition` is intrinsically a wide `match (state, event)` (complexity 30–80) and is exhaustively tested. An absolute gate would fight that architecture and reject more components as the catalog grows. The gate therefore runs in **regression mode**.\n\n- **Blessed entrypoints:** `cargo xcrap` (local human-readable report, never fails), `cargo xcrap --update-baseline` (regenerate the baseline), and `cargo xtask ci crap` (the CI gate). The gate is the last step of the full `cargo xci` pipeline, running immediately after `coverage` and reusing its merged `lcov.info` (no recompile). It is **not** in the fast profile (which skips coverage).\n- **Policy:** `cargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info --baseline .crap-baseline.json --fail-regression --min 30`. The gate fails only when a function **at or above CRAP 30 regresses** (more complexity or less coverage) versus the committed baseline. New functions are tolerated — the per-crate coverage thresholds already ensure new code is tested — and sub-threshold churn on simple functions is ignored. Net: the coverage gate guarantees new code is tested; the CRAP gate guarantees shipped code does not rot.\n- **Baseline:** `.crap-baseline.json` at the repo root is committed and complete (every function, no `--min`, so a function crossing the threshold is still caught). Regenerate it with `cargo xcrap --update-baseline` from CI's merged `lcov.info` (download the `coverage-lcov` artifact) whenever a **deliberate** complexity increase lands; commit the new baseline in that PR — a conscious \"I accept this\" checkpoint. cargo-crap's `--epsilon` (default 0.01) absorbs coverage float jitter. If the baseline is missing, the gate bootstraps it and passes with a notice.\n- **Pinned version:** cargo-crap is pinned to an exact version in `xtask::crap::CARGO_CRAP_VERSION` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (same convention as cargo-mutants). To bump, update both. Install locally with `cargo install cargo-crap --locked --version <version>` (or `cargo binstall cargo-crap`).\n- **Escape hatch:** the allow-glob list lives in `xtask/src/crap.rs` (`ALLOW_GLOBS`), each entry justified by a comment. Under `--workspace`, cargo-crap's `--exclude` is a no-op (it is relative to the ignored `--path`); path suppression must use `--allow`. `crates/ars-derive/**` is suppressed because llvm-cov never instruments proc-macro crates (they would score 0% coverage and pollute the baseline). Prefer fixing or refactoring over adding entries."
}
```

```
1	# CI / CD Specification
2	
3	This document defines the GitHub Actions pipelines that enforce the quality gates
4	from [13-policies.md](13-policies.md). Policy owns **what** (thresholds, naming,
5	directory structure); this file owns **how** (workflow YAML, caching, secrets,
6	branch protection).
7	
8	---
9	
10	## 1. PR Pipeline (Blocking)
11	
12	Every pull request MUST pass all jobs in this pipeline before merge is allowed.
13	The pipeline is intentionally front-loaded with cheap deterministic gates, then
14	widens after unit tests pass. Formatting runs first; workspace check and clippy
15	start from that baseline; unit tests wait for both; and release verification,
16	integration tests, adapter tests, coverage, and the feature-flag matrix all fan
17	out from the unit-test gate. This keeps early failures fast without needlessly
18	serializing independent expensive jobs.
19	
20	### 1.1 Formatting
21	
22	```yaml
23	fmt:
24	    name: Formatting
25	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
26	    steps:
27	        - uses: actions/checkout@v6
28	        - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
29	          with:
30	              components: rustfmt
31	        - run: cargo fmt --all --check
32	```
33	
34	### 1.2 Linting
35	
36	```yaml
37	clippy:
38	    name: Clippy
39	    needs: [fmt]
40	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
41	    steps:
42	        - uses: actions/checkout@v6
43	        - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
44	          with:
45	              components: clippy
46	        - uses: actions/cache@v5
47	          with:
48	              path: |
49	                  ~/.cargo/registry
50	                  ~/.cargo/git
51	                  target
52	              key: ${{ runner.os }}-cargo-clippy-${{ hashFiles('**/Cargo.lock') }}
53	              restore-keys: |
54	                  ${{ runner.os }}-cargo-
55	        - run: cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
56	        - name: Clippy (all features)
57	          run: cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings
58	```
59	
60	### 1.3 Unit Tests
61	
62	Cargo-based tests are orchestrated through `cargo xtest`, which wraps
63	`cargo nextest` for executable test binaries and keeps the staged CI test jobs
64	in sync with local full-suite runs. `cargo xtest` is test-only: compile checks
65	and cross-target verification remain part of the broader CI orchestration.
66	`cargo xtest` reports aggregate totals for the selected stage: total tests run
67	and total tests passed. `cargo xtest unit` follows the same `ars-i18n` backend
68	splitting strategy used by check/clippy: it first runs `cargo nextest run
69	--workspace --all-targets --all-features --exclude ars-i18n`, then runs
70	`ars-i18n` twice with `--all-targets --no-default-features`, once with the
71	`icu4x` backend feature set and once with the `web-intl` backend feature set.
72	
73	```yaml
74	unit:
75	    name: Unit Tests
76	    needs: [check, clippy]
77	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
78	    steps:

353	### 1.8 Mutual Exclusion Compile Error Tests
354	
355	The `icu4x` and `web-intl` features are mutually exclusive. Mutual exclusion
356	between `icu4x` and `web-intl` must be verified on every PR since breakage is
357	immediate.
358	
359	```yaml
360	mutual-exclusion:
361	    name: Mutual Exclusion Guard
362	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
363	    steps:
364	        - uses: actions/checkout@v6
365	        - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
366	        - name: Verify icu4x + web-intl fails to compile
367	          run: |
368	              if cargo check -p ars-i18n --no-default-features --features icu4x,web-intl 2>/dev/null; then
369	                echo "ERROR: icu4x + web-intl compiled successfully — mutual exclusion guard is broken"
370	                exit 1
371	              fi
372	              echo "OK: compile_error! fired as expected"
373	```
374	
375	---
376	
377	## 2. Coverage Pipeline
378	
379	### 2.1 Instrumentation
380	
381	Coverage is collected using `cargo-llvm-cov` with LLVM source-based
382	instrumentation. Output is in lcov format for Codecov compatibility. Native
383	coverage from `cargo-llvm-cov` is merged with experimental browser-test wasm
384	coverage for crates that contain `feature = "web"` / `target_arch = "wasm32"`
385	code paths and expose browser-executable tests.
386	
387	```yaml
388	coverage:
389	    name: Coverage
390	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
391	    needs: [unit]
392	    steps:
393	        - uses: actions/checkout@v6
394	        - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
395	          with:
396	              components: llvm-tools-preview
397	        - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@nightly
398	          with:
399	              targets: wasm32-unknown-unknown
400	              components: llvm-tools-preview
401	        - uses: actions/cache@v5
402	          with:
403	              path: |
404	                  ~/.cargo/registry
405	                  ~/.cargo/git
406	                  ~/.cache/wasm-pack
407	                  target
408	              key: ${{ runner.os }}-cargo-coverage-${{ hashFiles('**/Cargo.lock') }}
409	              restore-keys: |
410	                  ${{ runner.os }}-cargo-
411	        - uses: taiki-e/install-action@nextest
412	        - uses: taiki-e/install-action@cargo-llvm-cov
413	        - name: Install LLVM 22 for wasm coverage
414	          run: |
415	              wget -q https://apt.llvm.org/llvm.sh -O /tmp/llvm.sh
416	              chmod +x /tmp/llvm.sh
417	              sudo /tmp/llvm.sh 22
418	              sudo apt-get install -y clang-22 lld-22
419	        - run: cargo install wasm-bindgen-cli --locked --version 0.2.118
420	        - name: Configure wasm coverage clang
421	          run: echo "WASM_COVERAGE_CLANG=/usr/bin/clang-22" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
422	        - uses: ./.github/actions/install-dioxus-desktop-deps
423	        - name: Coverage
424	          run: cargo xtask ci coverage
425	```
426	
427	`cargo xtask ci coverage` performs three steps:
428	
429	1. Generate native workspace lcov with `cargo +nightly llvm-cov nextest
430	--branch`, so native coverage executes the same test binaries through
431	   `cargo-nextest` and exports real branch records in the lcov tracefile.
432	2. Generate per-package wasm lcov with `cargo xtask coverage wasm` for every
433	   browser-test crate in `xtask/src/coverage.rs :: default_wasm_coverage_targets()`.
434	   At the time of writing this includes:
435	    - `ars-dom` with `--features web`
436	    - `ars-i18n` with `--no-default-features --features web-intl`
437	    - `ars-leptos` with `--features csr`
438	    - `ars-dioxus` with `--features web`
439	    - `ars-test-harness-leptos`
440	    - `ars-test-harness-dioxus` with `--no-default-features --features ars-i18n/web-intl`
441	3. Merge the native and wasm lcov tracefiles with `cargo xtask coverage merge`
442	   so duplicate file records are unioned instead of double-counted.
```

  "new_string": "Every pull request MUST pass all jobs in this pipeline before merge is allowed.\nThe pipeline is intentionally front-loaded with cheap deterministic gates, then\nwidens once the code is proven to compile. Formatting runs first; clippy starts\nfrom that baseline. There is no separate `cargo check` job: `cargo clippy\n--workspace --all-targets --all-features` compiles a strict superset of what\n`cargo check` would, plus lints, so a dedicated check gate is redundant. Once\nclippy passes, compilation is guaranteed, so unit tests, release verification,\nintegration tests, adapter tests, coverage, and the feature-flag matrix all fan\nout from the **clippy** gate and run in parallel — clippy, not unit, is the\nfan-out point, so the independent expensive jobs no longer wait for unit-test\nresults they do not consume. This keeps early failures fast without needlessly\nserializing independent expensive jobs."
}

449	2. Generate per-package wasm lcov with `cargo xtask coverage wasm` for every
450	   browser-test crate in `xtask/src/coverage.rs :: default_wasm_coverage_targets()`.
451	   At the time of writing this includes:
452	    - `ars-dom` with `--features web`
453	    - `ars-i18n` with `--no-default-features --features web-intl`
454	    - `ars-leptos` with `--features csr`
455	    - `ars-dioxus` with `--features web`
456	    - `ars-test-harness-leptos`
457	    - `ars-test-harness-dioxus` with `--no-default-features --features ars-i18n/web-intl`
458	3. Merge the native and wasm lcov tracefiles with `cargo xtask coverage merge`
459	   so duplicate file records are unioned instead of double-counted.
460	
461	> **Note:** The wasm path uses the experimental `wasm-bindgen-test` coverage
462	> recipe on nightly Rust. It requires `llvm-tools-preview` on nightly,
463	> `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, `wasm-bindgen-test-runner`, and a wasm-capable
464	> `clang` whose LLVM major matches the nightly toolchain used to emit the
465	> intermediate `.ll` files. CI installs `clang-22` from `apt.llvm.org` and
466	> exports `WASM_COVERAGE_CLANG` so the recompile step does not accidentally
467	> pick an older system `clang`. The installed `wasm-bindgen-cli` /
468	> `wasm-bindgen-test-runner` version MUST match the workspace `wasm-bindgen`
469	> crate version in `Cargo.lock`. When the wasm exporter emits `BRDA` records
470	> with `taken = -`, `cargo xtask coverage` treats them as "no branch data"
471	> rather than failed branches, so merged branch thresholds are not artificially
472	> penalized by exporter omissions.
473	> Native coverage also requires nightly Rust and `cargo-nextest`, because
474	> branch coverage is unstable in `cargo-llvm-cov` and native execution is
475	> delegated through `cargo llvm-cov nextest`.

477	### 2.2 Per-Crate Thresholds

id: toolu_016H4QkfzYA3DcNpSd1N7BVa
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui/spec/testing/14-ci.md",
  "old_string": "> Native coverage also requires nightly Rust and `cargo-nextest`, because\n> branch coverage is unstable in `cargo-llvm-cov` and native execution is\n> delegated through `cargo llvm-cov nextest`.\n\n### 2.2 Per-Crate Thresholds",
  "new_string": "> Native coverage also requires nightly Rust and `cargo-nextest`, because\n> branch coverage is unstable in `cargo-llvm-cov` and native execution is\n> delegated through `cargo llvm-cov nextest`.\n\n### 2.1.1 CRAP Gate\n\nImmediately after coverage, the same job runs the CRAP gate\n([`cargo-crap`](https://crates.io/crates/cargo-crap), pinned to an exact\nversion) via `cargo xtask ci crap`. It reuses the merged `lcov.info` the\ncoverage step just produced, so it never recompiles — it only parses source and\nthe lcov tracefile. The CRAP score is `complexity² · (1 − coverage)³ +\ncomplexity`.\n\nThe gate runs in **regression mode**, not absolute-threshold mode:\n\n```\ncargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info \\\n    --baseline .crap-baseline.json --fail-regression --min 30 \\\n    --allow 'crates/ars-derive/**'\n```\n\nIt fails the job only when a function **at or above CRAP 30 regresses** (more\ncomplexity or less coverage) versus the committed `.crap-baseline.json`. New\nfunctions are tolerated — the per-crate coverage thresholds (§2.2) already\nensure new code is tested — and sub-threshold churn on simple functions is\nignored. The rationale: at 100% coverage `CRAP == cyclomatic complexity`, so an\nabsolute `--fail-above` threshold would reject every well-tested `Machine::transition`\n(an intrinsically wide `match (state, event)`), fighting the state-machine\narchitecture and worsening as the component catalog grows. Regression mode\ninstead guarantees shipped code does not rot while the coverage gate guarantees\nnew code is tested.\n\nUnder `--workspace`, cargo-crap's `--exclude` (relative to the ignored `--path`)\nis a no-op; path suppression therefore uses `--allow`. `crates/ars-derive/**` is\nsuppressed because llvm-cov never instruments proc-macro crates (they would\nscore 0% coverage and pollute the baseline). `.crap-baseline.json` is committed\nat the repo root, is complete (every function — so a function crossing the\nthreshold is still caught), and is regenerated with `cargo xcrap\n--update-baseline` from the `coverage-lcov` artifact when a deliberate\ncomplexity increase lands. If the baseline file is absent, the gate bootstraps\nit from the current lcov and passes with a notice. The pinned cargo-crap version\nis the single source of truth in `xtask::crap::CARGO_CRAP_VERSION` and the\nworkflow's `taiki-e/install-action` step.\n\n### 2.2 Per-Crate Thresholds"
}
```

755	    target
756	```
757	
758	### 4.3 Required Secrets
759	
760	| Secret          | Purpose                    | Required by       |
761	| --------------- | -------------------------- | ----------------- |
762	| `CODECOV_TOKEN` | Upload coverage to Codecov | Coverage pipeline |
763	
764	### 4.4 Branch Protection Rules
765	
766	The following status checks MUST be required on `main`:
767	
768	- `Formatting`
769	- `Workspace Check`
770	- `Clippy`
771	- `Unit Tests`
772	- `Release Verification`
773	- `Integration Tests`
774	- `Adapter Tests`
775	- `Feature Flag Check` (wrapper for all matrix combinations)
776	- `Coverage`
777	
778	### 4.5 Concurrency Groups
779	
780	PR pipelines use concurrency groups to cancel in-progress runs when a new push
781	arrives on the same branch:
782	
783	```yaml
784	concurrency:
785	    group: pr-${{ github.head_ref || github.run_id }}
786	    cancel-in-progress: true
787	```
788	
789	---

423	> CI enforcement: see [14-ci.md](14-ci.md#2-coverage-pipeline).
424	
425	**Snapshot count enforcement**: Every stateful component implementation file with more than two `State` variants MUST meet a per-variant floor. Rendered component modules (`component.rs`) require at least 3 snapshots per variant. Canonical `ars-components` machine modules require at least 1 snapshot per variant, focused on output-affecting connect-API attrs. Components with fewer fail the snapshot-count lint. The lint runs via `cargo xtask lint snapshot-count` and parses `*.snap` files in the test directory.
426	
427	> CI integration: see [14-ci.md](14-ci.md#24-snapshot-count-linting).
428	
429	**Property-based iteration count**: The `proptest` configuration in `Cargo.toml` MUST set
430	`PROPTEST_CASES=1000` as minimum. Nightly CI runs MAY increase to 10,000 for deeper coverage:
431	
432	> **Pipeline scope:** Property-based tests (`proptest`) run in the **nightly pipeline** ([14-ci.md section 3.1](14-ci.md#31-extended-property-based-testing)) with `PROPTEST_CASES=1000`. They are excluded from the PR pipeline via `#[ignore]` to keep PR feedback fast. The nightly job runs the ignored proptests for every core state-machine crate that defines them, including `ars-core` and `ars-components`.
433	
434	```toml
435	# In workspace Cargo.toml [profile.test]
436	[profile.test.package.ars-core]
437	# proptest picks up PROPTEST_CASES from env; default enforced in test harness
438	```

CI enforces a **regression**\npolicy, not an absolute ceiling: a function at or above CRAP 30 MUST NOT get\nworse (more complexity or less coverage) than the committed `.crap-baseline.json`\nwithout a deliberate baseline update. New functions are not gated here — the\nper-crate coverage floors above already require new code to be tested. An\nabsolute ceiling is intentionally avoided because at full coverage `CRAP` equals\ncyclomatic complexity, which would penalize the exhaustively-tested\n`Machine::transition` match that every stateful component requires.\n\n> CI enforcement: see [14-ci.md section 2.1.1](14-ci.md#211-crap-gate).\n\n**Snapshot count enforcement**:"

> AGENT

The implementation and the mandatory post-implementation audit are complete. Here's the consolidated summary for your review.

## What landed

**cargo-crap integration (regression-mode gate):**
- New `xtask/src/crap.rs` — three actions (`Gate` / `Report` / `UpdateBaseline`), pinned `CARGO_CRAP_VERSION = "0.2.2"`, `--allow` (not `--exclude`, which is a no-op under `--workspace`) for `ars-derive`, bootstrap-if-missing baseline, full `///` docs, 9 unit tests.
- `Step::Crap` runs right after `Coverage` in the full pipeline (reuses `lcov.info`, no recompile); excluded from the fast profile.
- Local entrypoints: `cargo xcrap` (report) and `cargo xcrap --update-baseline`.
- CI: `cargo-crap@0.2.2` install + CRAP gate step + always-on `coverage-lcov` artifact appended to the coverage job.
- `.crap-baseline.json` committed (native-generated **safe floor** — CI's merged coverage ≥ native ⇒ no false regressions).
- Gate verified empirically: fresh-vs-fresh → **0 regressed, exit 0**; sub-threshold churn ignored (`--min 30`); above-threshold regression caught; new functions tolerated.

**CI speedup + check removal:**
- `check` job deleted; `cargo check` is a strict subset of `clippy --all-targets --all-features`.
- Fan-out rewired from `needs: [unit]` → `needs: [clippy]` (one full compile stage off the critical path).

## Audit findings (all landed in this PR, no deferral)

- **Phase 1 (drift):** `spec/testing/14-ci.md` is the authoritative CI spec and had drifted — synced the front-loading prose, `check`→`clippy` references, the `unit`/fan-out/`adapter-parity`/`mutual-exclusion` `needs` graph, added a **§2.1.1 CRAP Gate** section + the gate step/artifact in the coverage YAML, and removed `Workspace Check` from required-status-checks (with a migration note).
- **Phase 2 (anything missing):** added a **Change-risk (CRAP) enforcement** policy note to `spec/testing/13-policies.md`; confirmed no dev-setup tool list, CHANGELOG, or ci.yml-parity test needed updating; AGENTS.md CRAP section rewritten for regression mode.
- **Phase 3 (tests):** added `error_display_is_actionable`; `build_args`/consts/allow-globs fully covered. 183 xtask tests pass, clippy `-D warnings` clean, fmt OK, spec-snippet gate green.

## Two things to flag

1. **Branch protection (manual, repo-admin):** when this merges, remove the `Workspace Check` required status check in GitHub settings or merges will hang on a job that no longer reports. (Documented in the 14-ci migration note; goes in the PR body.)
2. **`.crap-baseline.json` is 2.4 MB** (complete baseline — required so a function *crossing* the threshold is still caught). It regenerates infrequently but produces large diffs when it does. I can floor it (e.g. `--min 15`, ~10× smaller) at the cost of missing single-PR jumps from far-below to ≥30 if you'd prefer — say the word.
