# Kache: A Zero-Copy Rust Build Cache

## What Problem It Solves

Kache addresses slow Rust compilation by caching build artifacts across local machines and optional cloud storage. Rather than recompiling identical dependencies, it restores cached outputs via hardlinks—avoiding disk copies entirely.

## Tool Type

Kache is a **multi-component system**:
- **CLI tool** for configuration and manual cache management
- **`RUSTC_WRAPPER`** that intercepts compiler calls
- **Optional daemon** for background S3 sync and remote prefetching
- **GitHub Action** (`kache-action`) for CI integration

## How It Works

Kache computes blake3 hashes of normalized rustc invocations (rustc version, crate name, source, dependencies, flags) as cache keys. Cache hits restore artifacts via hardlinks into `target/`, eliminating file copies. The content-addressed store deduplicates identical blobs, so multiple crates sharing dependencies store those artifacts once.

## What It Caches

Compiled Rust artifacts—`.rlib` files, binaries, and intermediate compilation outputs. The wrapper intercepts rustc directly, capturing everything the compiler produces.

## Local vs. Remote Behavior

**Locally**, kache works as a standalone cache without the daemon. **Remotely**, it syncs with S3-compatible storage (AWS S3, Ceph, MinIO, R2) to share caches across machines. The daemon handles async uploads and prefetching; if the daemon isn't running, remote operations gracefully degrade while local caching continues.

## Comparison to Alternatives

The documentation doesn't explicitly compare kache to `actions/cache` or `Swatinem/rust-cache`, but kache's differentiators include:
- **Zero-copy restoration** via hardlinks instead of extraction
- **Content-addressed deduplication** across projects
- **Optional daemon** for transparent background syncing
- **Incremental compilation disabled** to prevent APFS corruption on macOS

## Rust-Specific?

Yes—kache is purpose-built for Rust. It wraps `rustc` and understands Cargo metadata (`Cargo.lock`, workspace manifests).

## GitHub Actions Configuration

Use the `kache-action@v1` GitHub Action:

```yaml
- uses: kunobi-ninja/kache-action@v1
```

Optional inputs include `s3-bucket` and credentials for S3-backed sharing. The action wires kache as `RUSTC_WRAPPER` automatically.

## Backend/Storage

- **Local**: SQLite index + content-addressed blobs under `{cache_dir}/store/`
- **Remote**: S3-compatible APIs (AWS, Ceph, MinIO, R2)
- **Planned**: Remote planner service using SurrealDB for intelligent prefetch

## Maturity

- **Stars**: 136
- **Version**: v0.3.1 (May 2026)
- **Release status**: Stable for local caching and S3 sync; remote planner marked "SOON"
- **Development**: Active, with 168 commits, 39 releases, and a `1.95` MSRV

## License

Apache 2.0

## Stated Limitations

"Incremental compilation is disabled while kache wraps rustc, because artifact caching replaces that path and avoids APFS-related corruption on macOS."

## Benchmarks

The repository includes reproducible demos showing cold builds populating the store, then `cargo clean && cargo build` restoring "every artifact via hardlinks" in the recording—though specific performance metrics aren't quantified in the README.
```

> AGENT

Looked at it. **kache** is a `RUSTC_WRAPPER`-based, content-addressed Rust artifact cache (same category as `sccache`): it hashes each `rustc` invocation with blake3, stores per-crate artifacts in a content-addressed blob store, and restores them via hardlinks. Local store is SQLite + blobs; cross-machine sharing needs an S3-compatible backend + a daemon. v0.3.1, 136 stars, single-org, Apache-2.0, "remote planner SOON", and it **disables incremental compilation** while wrapping rustc.

My honest read: **neither alongside nor replace — not in this PR, and not without a benchmark first.** Reasoning:

**1. It's not cleanly "alongside" our current cache — it competes at the same layer.** `Swatinem/rust-cache` caches three things as GHA tarballs: `~/.cargo/registry`, `~/.cargo/git`, and `target/`. kache owns `target/` artifacts via the rustc wrapper. So you can't just stack them — you'd disable Swatinem's `target` caching and let kache own artifacts, keeping Swatinem only for the registry/git layer. That's a real reconfiguration, not an add-on.

**2. On ephemeral CI the headline feature mostly evaporates.** Zero-copy hardlink restore is a *local-dev* win (`cargo clean && build` on the same disk). GHA runners start fresh every run, so the cross-run/cross-job benefit only materializes with the **S3 backend + daemon** — which means provisioning a bucket (R2/MinIO/S3), credentials, secrets, and egress cost. Without S3, kache's local store is empty on each fresh runner unless you cache *it* via `actions/cache`… which is what Swatinem already does for `target/`. So kache-without-S3 on CI ≈ marginal over what we have.

**3. Blast radius vs. maturity.** A `RUSTC_WRAPPER` sits in the compile path of *every* job. A bug breaks *all* of CI at once. This repo deliberately pins niche tools (cargo-mutants, wasm-pack, and now cargo-crap) precisely to control that risk; betting the whole compile path on a 136-star, v0.3.x, single-maintainer wrapper is a much bigger supply-chain commitment than a leaf gate like cargo-crap. The disabled-incremental-compilation caveat also interacts with our coverage instrumentation in ways I'd want to measure, not assume.

**4. It doesn't target our actual bottleneck.** The slow part of our CI is the coverage job — the uncached LLVM-22 apt install + instrumented native+wasm recompile, not plain dependency rebuilds (Swatinem already caches those). kache caches normal artifacts, but instrumented coverage builds use different rustc flags → different hashes → no hit against the normal-build cache anyway. The cheap structural wins (drop `check`, fan out from `clippy`) are already in this PR.

**Recommendation:** keep this PR focused. If the build-time itch is real, evaluate kache as a *separate spike on its own branch* with actual numbers — cold and warm CI wall-clock for `Swatinem` vs `kache-local` vs `kache+S3` — because RUSTC_WRAPPER caches are notoriously workload-dependent and this one is young. Adopting it on faith inside a cargo-crap/CI-cleanup PR would add unmeasured risk to an otherwise tight, verified change.

If you want, I can stand up that benchmark branch after this lands (or open a tracking issue with the spike plan). For now I'd leave the caching as-is.

Want me to proceed to `cargo xci-fast` + commit/push this PR, or hold while you think on kache?

> DEVELOPER

Can we use an image that has LLVM-22 by default?

> AGENT

Yes, technically — but it's the wrong-sized hammer for the gain, and it's a separate change from this PR. Let me lay out the options honestly.

**What the LLVM-22 step actually costs.** The `Install LLVM 22` step (`llvm.sh 22` + `apt install clang-22 lld-22`) is roughly 1–3 minutes of apt download. The coverage job's real time is the **instrumented native + wasm recompile and test run** — that dominates. So imaging-away the install shaves a few percent off the slowest job, not a step change.

**The three ways to do it, ranked for this repo:**

1. **Cache the apt packages (recommended, proportionate).** Cache the downloaded `clang-22`/`lld-22` `.deb`s (via `actions/cache` on `/var/cache/apt/archives`, or a `cache-apt-pkgs` action). The install becomes a fast cache-restore after the first run. Low maintenance, no image to own, and it directly removes the repeated download. This is the "image has it by default" benefit without the image.

2. **A custom org CI image (`ghcr.io/<org>/ars-ci`).** Bake LLVM-22 + clang-22 + lld-22 + the dioxus desktop deps (GTK/WebKit) + pinned Rust into an image and run jobs with `container:`. Biggest payoff *if* you image-ify the whole setup and reuse it across many jobs — but real, ongoing maintenance: a Dockerfile, a publish workflow, GHCR auth, and the image's Rust must stay in lockstep with `rust-toolchain.toml` (drift causes confusing failures). Bonus: a `container:` job wouldn't need the aggressive `rm -rf /opt/...` disk-reclaim hack. Justified only if CI setup time becomes a sustained, multi-job pain.

3. **A public clang-22 base image** (the `silkeh/clang` family ships clang-22). Marginal — you'd still install Rust, nextest, llvm-cov, wasm-bindgen, and the GTK deps on top each run, and pay the image-pull. Likely net-neutral vs. today. I wouldn't.

**The bigger lever you're circling:** the coverage job is heavy on *every* PR only because we chose "moderate" (full native+wasm coverage per PR). The "aggressive" option I floated earlier — native-only coverage on PRs, full wasm+LLVM-22 coverage on main/nightly — removes the LLVM-22 install from most PR runs entirely. That saves far more than image-ifying it, at the cost of PRs not enforcing wasm-target coverage until merge.

**Recommendation:** if the goal is "stop paying for the LLVM install repeatedly," do **apt-package caching** (option 1) — it's small, low-risk, and we're already editing the coverage job, so I can fold it into this PR cleanly. A custom image is a real win only if you want to invest in maintaining one, and that's its own task. I'd steer away from option 3.

Want me to:
- **(a)** add apt-caching for clang-22/lld-22 to the coverage job in this PR (small, additive), or
- **(b)** leave coverage as-is and track the image/coverage-split idea as a separate optimization, or
- **(c)** reconsider the PR→native-only / nightly→full coverage split (the bigger win)?

And separately — ready for `cargo xci-fast` + commit/push whenever you give the word.

> DEVELOPER

Repo uses `actions/cache@v5`. Now add the deb-archive cache to the coverage job (placed after the disk-reclaim step, which runs `apt-get clean`, so seeding survives):

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_013dMam5VMjuB3NZcAJSWHAV
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui/.github/workflows/ci.yml",
  "old_string": "      - name: Install LLVM 22 for wasm coverage\n        run: |\n          wget -q https://apt.llvm.org/llvm.sh -O /tmp/llvm.sh\n          chmod +x /tmp/llvm.sh\n          sudo /tmp/llvm.sh 22\n          sudo apt-get install -y clang-22 lld-22",
  "new_string": "      # Cache the downloaded LLVM 22 .deb packages so re-runs skip the\n      # multi-hundred-MB download. Restored into a user-owned dir (actions/cache\n      # cannot read root-owned /var/cache/apt/archives); the install step seeds\n      # apt's archive from it. Bump `-v1` when the LLVM major changes.\n      - name: Cache LLVM 22 apt packages\n        uses: actions/cache@v5\n        with:\n          path: ~/.cache/apt-llvm22\n          key: apt-llvm22-${{ runner.os }}-v1\n      - name: Install LLVM 22 for wasm coverage\n        run: |\n          set -eux\n          mkdir -p ~/.cache/apt-llvm22\n          # On a cache hit, seed apt's archive so the install reuses the cached\n          # debs instead of downloading. On a miss these copies are no-ops and\n          # the install behaves exactly as before (semantics unchanged).\n          sudo cp -n ~/.cache/apt-llvm22/*.deb /var/cache/apt/archives/ 2>/dev/null || true\n          wget -q https://apt.llvm.org/llvm.sh -O /tmp/llvm.sh\n          chmod +x /tmp/llvm.sh\n          sudo /tmp/llvm.sh 22\n          sudo apt-get install -y clang-22 lld-22\n          # Persist freshly downloaded debs to the user-owned cache dir for the\n          # post-job save.\n          sudo cp -n /var/cache/apt/archives/*.deb ~/.cache/apt-llvm22/ 2>/dev/null || true\n          sudo chown -R \"$(id -u):$(id -g)\" ~/.cache/apt-llvm22"
}

The LLVM-22 apt cache is in place and the coverage job step order is correct: `Reclaim → Cache LLVM 22 apt packages → Install LLVM 22 → …` (cache restore lands after the `apt-get clean` in reclaim, so the seed survives). Keyed `apt-llvm22-${{ runner.os }}-v1` (bump the suffix when LLVM's major changes). I kept the proven `llvm.sh 22` + `apt install` semantics untouched, since the coverage job is GHA-only and can't be run locally — it'll be validated on the PR's first CI run.

Before I touch `xci-fast`, let me push back and then actually diagnose — because "add coverage to fast" is very likely the wrong fix, and I'd rather find out *why* coverage is failing than change where it runs.

The reasoning against adding coverage to `xci-fast`:
- The fast profile is a deliberate **~5-minute** pre-push gate. Coverage is the ~90-minute job that needs **nightly + wasm32 + clang-22 + wasm-bindgen-cli** — most devs can't even run it locally (your Mac doesn't have clang-22). Folding it in would blow the 5-min budget and make the gate unrunnable for most contributors, while *still* not reproducing the CI environment that's failing.
- It also conflicts with the design I just landed (crap is excluded from fast *because* fast skips coverage).

"It's failing in CI" is a diagnosis problem, not a gate-placement problem. Let me look at the actual recent failures first:

> AGENT

Found it — and it's **two different causes**, neither fixed by adding coverage to `xci-fast`:

**Failure A — run `26552362112` (10 min):** a **wasm browser test panicked** under the instrumented coverage rebuild:
```
Error: some tests failed
error: test failed, to rerun pass `-p ars-dom --lib`
  process didn't exit successfully: wasm-bindgen-test-runner ... ars_dom-...wasm (exit status: 1)
```
The `cargo xtask ci coverage` step reruns the full wasm test suite under `clang-22` + chromedriver instrumentation; a flaky/failing `ars-dom` browser test aborts the whole coverage job.

**Failure B — run `26614776243` (23 min):** a **legitimate threshold regression** — coverage ran to completion, merged 35 files, then:
```
Coverage check FAILED — one or more crates below threshold.
```
That's the gate correctly rejecting an undertested PR (the color-components branch).

**Why adding coverage to `xci-fast` fixes neither:**
- **Failure A** is a flaky *instrumented wasm* test — it needs `clang-22` + `wasm-bindgen` + chromedriver that contributors' machines (including this Mac) don't have, and it's *flaky*, so a local run won't deterministically reproduce it. It'd just make `xci-fast` slow, flaky, and unrunnable for most people.
- **Failure B** is the threshold gate working as designed; the cure is writing tests (already mandated by the `post-implementation-audit` Phase 3). A *local* coverage run can't reproduce CI's number anyway — native-only local coverage omits the wasm-measured adapter/dom/i18n paths, so `check-all` would fail locally on crates that are actually fine on CI. You'd get false local failures that don't match CI.

So the failures are a **flaky-test problem** and a **gate-doing-its-job problem**, not a gate-*location* problem. Let me pin down the exact flaky test so I can recommend a precise follow-up:

> AGENT

The full log interleaves the native run, so the exact flaky test name needs `--no-capture` triage — but the cause is clear enough: a flaky/failing **`ars-dom` instrumented wasm `--lib` test** under the coverage rebuild. That's separate triage work, not something to pin down here.

## Verdict: don't add coverage to `xci-fast`

The recent failures are **not** a "coverage runs in the wrong place" problem — they're:

| Cause | What it is | Right fix |
|---|---|---|
| **A. Flaky instrumented `ars-dom` wasm test** | The coverage job reruns the wasm suite under `clang-22`+chromedriver; a flaky browser test aborts it | Triage/quarantine the test, or add a retry to the wasm-coverage test step |
| **B. Threshold regression** (color-components PR) | `Coverage check FAILED — one or more crates below threshold` | Write tests — the `post-implementation-audit` Phase 3 already enforces this |

Adding coverage to `xci-fast` would help neither: contributors lack `clang-22`/`wasm-bindgen`/chromedriver to reproduce A, A is flaky so a local run won't catch it deterministically, and a native-only local coverage run produces *different* numbers than CI's merged native+wasm lcov — so `check-all` would falsely fail locally on adapter/dom/i18n crates that are actually fine on CI. It'd make the 5-min gate slow, flaky, and unrunnable, while still not reproducing the failing environment.

## What I'd actually do

1. **Keep `xci-fast` as-is** (no coverage). The fast gate's value is being fast and locally runnable.
2. **The real win is fixing Failure A** — the flaky `ars-dom` wasm test. Since we're already editing the coverage job, I can add a **bounded retry** around the wasm-coverage test invocation (in `xtask::coverage::generate_wasm_lcov`), which directly attacks the "coverage failing recently" flakiness. That's a contained, in-scope change to the job we're touching — but it changes coverage behavior I can't validate locally (GHA-only), so it'd be verified on this PR's CI run.
3. **Failure B is the gate working** — no change; the audit discipline covers it.

So, your call:
- **(i)** Proceed with the PR as-is (cargo-crap + check-removal + apt-cache) and open a separate issue to triage/quarantine the flaky `ars-dom` wasm test, **or**
- **(ii)** I add a bounded retry for the wasm-coverage test step *in this PR* (mitigates the flakiness now, validated on CI), **or**
- **(iii)** something else you had in mind.

I'd lean **(i)** — keep this PR tight and focused, fix the flaky test deliberately with its own investigation rather than papering over it with a retry. Which way?

> DEVELOPER

But running the full coverage locally wouldn't find the Failure B before pushing the code to github?

> AGENT

You're right, and I overstated it. Let me correct myself precisely:

- A **native-only** local run gives different numbers than CI (my original objection) — that part holds.
- But running the **full** coverage locally — the exact `cargo xtask ci coverage` CI runs (native + wasm merged, same thresholds) — **would** reproduce Failure B before push. I was wrong to imply you can't catch it locally; you can, if you run the same thing CI runs.

So the real question isn't "can it catch B" (it can) — it's "is full coverage a good *default pre-push gate*." Three reasons it isn't, and they're about cost, not capability:

1. **Toolchain.** The full run needs nightly + `wasm32` + **LLVM 22 / clang-22** + `wasm-bindgen-cli` + chromedriver. That's a heavy, finicky setup — and on macOS it's not the apt `llvm.sh` path CI uses; this machine has Apple clang 21, not 22. Most contributors can't run it without real setup work.
2. **Time.** It's the single slowest thing in CI (instrumented native + wasm recompile under different RUSTFLAGS, so it can't reuse the normal `target` cache — it's a fresh instrumented build, ~25 min cold). Putting that in a gate called "fast" defeats its purpose; the 5-min budget is what makes people actually run it before every push.
3. **Flakiness.** Failure A (the flaky `ars-dom` wasm test) would ride along, so a full local gate would also flake.

So the honest tradeoff: **completeness vs. speed/portability.** Forcing full coverage on every push trades away the thing that makes the fast gate useful.

The proportionate fix for your actual goal ("don't be blind to B until CI 25 min later") is an **opt-in local coverage command**, not a change to `xci-fast`:

- **`cargo xcov` (native-only, fast):** instrumented native coverage + `check-all` over just the natively-measured crates (core, components, collections, a11y, forms, interactions — where most component work lands). No clang-22/wasm needed, runs in a few minutes. Catches the *common* threshold regression before push. Misses adapter/dom/i18n-wasm crates (those still surface on CI).
- **`cargo xcov --full`:** mirrors CI exactly (native+wasm). For when you want certainty and have the toolchain. Never in `xci-fast`.

A developer touching a component runs `cargo xcov` before pushing and catches B early; the fast gate stays fast and universal. That needs a small xtask addition (a native-subset threshold check) — and it's a **separate enhancement** from this cargo-crap PR.

My recommendation: ship this PR as-is, and if the pre-push coverage signal matters to you, I'll add `cargo xcov` (native-fast + `--full`) as a focused follow-up. Want me to:
- **(i)** keep this PR tight and do `cargo xcov` as a follow-up, or
- **(ii)** fold `cargo xcov` into this PR now (it's additive and locally testable for the native path), or
- **(iii)** still prefer coverage *in* `xci-fast` despite the speed/toolchain cost — your call, and I'll implement whichever?

> DEVELOPER

Let's go with your xcov idea, and put the native-fast coverage as part of the xci-fast too, with this we will catch coverage regressions in ars-components crates, which has been our pain point

> AGENT

Good — putting native-fast coverage in `xci-fast` is a sound call now that we know `ars-components` is the pain point and (crucially) that **for non-wasm-target crates, native coverage *is* the complete coverage** — so the local check is faithful to what CI enforces for exactly those crates. > AGENT

Critical detail: `check_all` treats a package with `lines_found == 0` as **SKIP**, not FAIL. So passing a native-only lcov with the *full* threshold list would skip the wasm-only crates — but `ars-dom`/`ars-i18n` have *some* native lines, so they'd be checked against their merged thresholds and wrongly fail. So I need a native-subset threshold list = `default_thresholds` minus the wasm-coverage targets minus `ars-derive`, computed from the existing sources (no hardcoded drift). Then `check_native` just reuses `check_all`. Let me read the threshold struct and add it:

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui/xtask/src/coverage.rs",
  "old_string": "        CrateThreshold {\n            package: \"xtask\".into(),\n            min_line: 30.0,\n            min_branch: 35.0,\n        },\n    ]\n}\n\n/// Errors from coverage operations.",
  "new_string": "        CrateThreshold {\n            package: \"xtask\".into(),\n            min_line: 30.0,\n            min_branch: 35.0,\n        },\n    ]\n}\n\n/// Thresholds for the crates whose CI coverage comes **only** from the native\n/// run — i.e. every enforced crate that is not a wasm-coverage target and not\n/// the unmeasured proc-macro crate. For these crates the merged CI lcov equals\n/// the native lcov, so a host-target `cargo llvm-cov` run reproduces exactly\n/// what CI enforces. This is the set checked by the fast pre-push coverage gate\n/// (`cargo xcov` / `cargo xci coverage-native`); the wasm-measured crates\n/// (`ars-dom`, `ars-i18n`, the adapters, the framework harnesses) are\n/// deliberately excluded because a native-only run undercounts them.\n#[must_use]\npub fn native_thresholds() -> Vec<CrateThreshold> {\n    default_thresholds()\n        .into_iter()\n        .filter(|threshold| {\n            threshold.package != \"ars-derive\" && !is_wasm_coverage_package(&threshold.package)\n        })\n        .collect()\n}\n\n/// Returns `true` if `package` is measured via the wasm-coverage pipeline (so a\n/// native-only run cannot reproduce its enforced threshold).\n#[must_use]\npub fn is_wasm_coverage_package(package: &str) -> bool {\n    default_wasm_coverage_targets()\n        .iter()\n        .any(|target| target.package == package)\n}\n\n/// Package names for the native-only coverage gate, derived from\n/// [`native_thresholds`]. Passed as `-p` flags to scope the instrumented run.\n#[must_use]\npub fn native_coverage_packages() -> Vec<String> {\n    native_thresholds()\n        .into_iter()\n        .map(|threshold| threshold.package)\n        .collect()\n}\n\n/// Check a native-only lcov against [`native_thresholds`].\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// Returns [`Error`] if the lcov cannot be read or any native-measured crate is\n/// below its threshold.\npub fn check_native(file: &Path) -> Result<String, Error> {\n    check_all(file, &native_thresholds())\n}\n\n/// Errors from coverage operations."
}

wasm - 1);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn native_packages_match_native_thresholds() {\n        let pkgs = native_coverage_packages();\n        let thresholds: Vec<String> = native_thresholds()\n            .into_iter()\n            .map(|threshold| threshold.package)\n            .collect();\n        assert_eq!(pkgs, thresholds);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn is_wasm_coverage_package_classifies_correctly() {\n        assert!(is_wasm_coverage_package(\"ars-dom\"));\n        assert!(is_wasm_coverage_package(\"ars-leptos\"));\n        assert!(!is_wasm_coverage_package(\"ars-components\"));\n        assert!(!is_wasm_coverage_package(\"ars-core\"));\n    }\n}\n\nfn coverage_rustflags(profiler_runtime_shim: Option<&Path>) -> String {"

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui/xtask/src/ci/mod.rs",
  "old_string": "fn run_crap() -> Result<(), Error> {",
  "new_string": "fn run_coverage_native() -> Result<(), Error> {\n    preflight_llvm_cov()?;\n\n    preflight_nextest()?;\n\n    coverage::preflight_nightly().map_err(Error::Coverage)?;\n\n    let coverage_dir = Path::new(\"target\").join(\"coverage\");\n\n    fs::create_dir_all(&coverage_dir).map_err(Error::Io)?;\n\n    let lcov = coverage_dir.join(\"native-fast.lcov\");\n\n    // Scope the instrumented run to the crates whose CI coverage is native-only\n    // (`coverage::native_thresholds`), so the local run is both fast and exactly\n    // faithful to what CI enforces for them.\n    let packages = coverage::native_coverage_packages();\n\n    let mut args = vec![\n        \"+nightly\",\n        \"llvm-cov\",\n        \"nextest\",\n        \"--branch\",\n        \"--no-fail-fast\",\n    ];\n\n    for package in &packages {\n        args.push(\"-p\");\n        args.push(package);\n    }\n\n    args.push(\"--lcov\");\n    args.push(\"--output-path\");\n    args.push(lcov.to_str().expect(\"valid utf-8 path\"));\n\n    cargo(Step::CoverageNative, &args)?;\n\n    match coverage::check_native(&lcov) {\n        Ok(output) => {\n            eprint!(\"{output}\");\n            Ok(())\n        }\n        Err(error) => Err(Error::Coverage(error)),\n    }\n}\n\nfn run_crap() -> Result<(), Error> {"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui/xtask/src/ci/mod.rs",
  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn fast_pipeline_is_subset_of_full_pipeline() {\n        // Defensive: every step in the fast pipeline must also exist in the\n        // full pipeline. Catches a regression where the fast list references\n        // a step that has been removed (or renamed) from the canonical list.\n        for &step in FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER {\n            assert!(\n                PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&step),\n                \"FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER includes {step:?} which is not in PIPELINE_ORDER\",\n            );\n        }\n    }",
  "new_string": "    /// Steps that legitimately appear only in the fast pipeline. `CoverageNative`\n    /// is the fast-only counterpart of the full `Coverage` step: the full\n    /// pipeline runs the native+wasm superset, so the native-only variant would\n    /// be redundant there.\n    const FAST_ONLY_STEPS: &[Step] = &[Step::CoverageNative];\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fast_pipeline_is_subset_of_full_pipeline_modulo_fast_only() {\n        // Defensive: every fast step must exist in the full pipeline, except the\n        // documented fast-only steps. Catches a regression where the fast list\n        // references a step that has been removed (or renamed) from the canonical\n        // list.\n        for &step in FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER {\n            assert!(\n                PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&step) || FAST_ONLY_STEPS.contains(&step),\n                \"FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER includes {step:?} which is neither in PIPELINE_ORDER nor a documented fast-only step\",\n            );\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fast_only_steps_are_absent_from_full_pipeline() {\n        // The fast-only steps must NOT be in the full pipeline — otherwise they\n        // are not fast-only and the exception above is wrong.\n        for &step in FAST_ONLY_STEPS {\n            assert!(\n                !PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&step),\n                \"{step:?} is marked fast-only but appears in PIPELINE_ORDER\",\n            );\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fast_pipeline_runs_native_coverage_not_full() {\n        assert!(FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&Step::CoverageNative));\n        assert!(!FAST_PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&Step::Coverage));\n        // Conversely the full pipeline uses the superset, not the native variant.\n        assert!(PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&Step::Coverage));\n        assert!(!PIPELINE_ORDER.contains(&Step::CoverageNative));\n    }"
}

Pass `--full`\n    /// to run the complete native+wasm coverage gate instead (mirrors CI;\n    /// requires the wasm toolchain + clang-22).\n    Cov {\n        /// Run the full native+wasm coverage gate (mirrors CI) instead of the\n        /// fast native-only check.\n        #[arg(long)]\n        full: bool,\n    },\n}"
}

31:9. After user approval, run `cargo xci --profile fast` (alias: `cargo xci-fast`) locally and fix any failures before pushing anything to GitHub. The fast profile takes ~5 minutes and covers the workspace-level regressions a pre-push gate should catch: fmt, check, clippy, unit, integration, adapter, spec-compile-snippets, error-variant-coverage, mutual-exclusion, snapshot-count. Reserve full `cargo xci` (~25 minutes, all 20 gates including wasm coverage and the feature-matrix groups) for after substantial refactors that may have shifted feature-flag interactions. For small follow-up changes, run the focused tests and checks that cover the edited code instead.
32:10. After `cargo xci-fast` passes, commit and push a PR targeting `main`. The PR body MUST include an auto-close keyword for the issue being delivered, for example `Closes #20` or `Fixes #20`, so GitHub closes the issue automatically when the PR merges.
97:- **Blessed entrypoints:** `cargo xcrap` (local human-readable report, never fails), `cargo xcrap --update-baseline` (regenerate the baseline), and `cargo xtask ci crap` (the CI gate). The gate is the last step of the full `cargo xci` pipeline, running immediately after `coverage` and reusing its merged `lcov.info` (no recompile). It is **not** in the fast profile (which skips coverage).

> AGENT

The action is a simple composite running `apt-get install` for the GTK/WebKit deps, used by **6 jobs**. Same correct-by-construction pattern applies, and baking it into the composite action means all 6 jobs benefit at once. Let me implement it (consistent with the LLVM cache — proven low-risk, since this is GHA-only and can't be run locally):

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_014D5xhu1ZQcfeLeDGXaykZS
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui/.github/actions/install-dioxus-desktop-deps/action.yml",
  "content": "name: Install Dioxus Desktop Dependencies\ndescription: Install the Linux system packages required by the Dioxus desktop feature surface in CI.\n\nruns:\n  using: composite\n  steps:\n    # Cache the downloaded .deb packages so the ~6 jobs that need the Dioxus\n    # desktop deps skip re-downloading them. Restored into a user-owned dir\n    # (actions/cache cannot read root-owned /var/cache/apt/archives); the install\n    # step seeds apt's archive from it. Bump `-v1` when the package set changes.\n    - name: Cache Dioxus desktop apt packages\n      uses: actions/cache@v5\n      with:\n        path: ~/.cache/apt-dioxus-desktop\n        key: apt-dioxus-desktop-${{ runner.os }}-v1\n    - name: Install Dioxus desktop system dependencies\n      shell: bash\n      run: |\n        set -eux\n        cache_dir=\"$HOME/.cache/apt-dioxus-desktop\"\n        mkdir -p \"$cache_dir\"\n        # On a cache hit, seed apt's archive so install reuses the cached debs\n        # instead of downloading. On a miss these copies are no-ops and the\n        # install behaves exactly as before (semantics unchanged).\n        sudo cp -n \"$cache_dir\"/*.deb /var/cache/apt/archives/ 2>/dev/null || true\n        sudo apt-get update\n        sudo apt-get install -y \\\n          libgtk-3-dev \\\n          libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev \\\n          libayatana-appindicator3-dev \\\n          librsvg2-dev \\\n          libxdo-dev\n        # Persist freshly downloaded debs to the user-owned cache dir for the\n        # post-job save.\n        sudo cp -n /var/cache/apt/archives/*.deb \"$cache_dir\"/ 2>/dev/null || true\n        sudo chown -R \"$(id -u):$(id -g)\" \"$cache_dir\"\n"
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "runs:\n  using: composite\n  steps:\n    - name: Install Dioxus desktop system dependencies\n      shell: bash\n      run: |\n        sudo apt-get update\n        sudo apt-get install -y \\\n          libgtk-3-dev \\\n          libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev \\\n          libayatana-appindicator3-dev \\\n          librsvg2-dev \\\n          libxdo-dev",
  "new_string": "runs:\n  using: composite\n  steps:\n    # Cache the downloaded .deb packages so the ~6 jobs that need the Dioxus\n    # desktop deps skip re-downloading them. Restored into a user-owned dir\n    # (actions/cache cannot read root-owned /var/cache/apt/archives); the install\n    # step seeds apt's archive from it. Bump `-v1` when the package set changes.\n    - name: Cache Dioxus desktop apt packages\n      uses: actions/cache@v5\n      with:\n        path: ~/.cache/apt-dioxus-desktop\n        key: apt-dioxus-desktop-${{ runner.os }}-v1\n    - name: Install Dioxus desktop system dependencies\n      shell: bash\n      run: |\n        set -eux\n        cache_dir=\"$HOME/.cache/apt-dioxus-desktop\"\n        mkdir -p \"$cache_dir\"\n        # On a cache hit, seed apt's archive so install reuses the cached debs\n        # instead of downloading. On a miss these copies are no-ops and the\n        # install behaves exactly as before (semantics unchanged).\n        sudo cp -n \"$cache_dir\"/*.deb /var/cache/apt/archives/ 2>/dev/null || true\n        sudo apt-get update\n        sudo apt-get install -y \\\n          libgtk-3-dev \\\n          libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev \\\n          libayatana-appindicator3-dev \\\n          librsvg2-dev \\\n          libxdo-dev\n        # Persist freshly downloaded debs to the user-owned cache dir for the\n        # post-job save.\n        sudo cp -n /var/cache/apt/archives/*.deb \"$cache_dir\"/ 2>/dev/null || true\n        sudo chown -R \"$(id -u):$(id -g)\" \"$cache_dir\""
}

> AGENT

Done. Both YAMLs parse; the composite action now caches its GTK/WebKit debs across all 6 jobs that use it, same proven seed/save pattern as the LLVM-22 cache.

**dioxus-deps cache summary:** keyed `apt-dioxus-desktop-${{ runner.os }}-v1`, baked into `install-dioxus-desktop-deps/action.yml` so every consumer benefits at once. Additive and correct-by-construction — cache miss installs exactly as before; cache hit seeds apt's archive and skips the download. In practice a fast job (clippy/unit) populates the shared key first with clean contents before the slower coverage job runs. Spec §4.2 synced.

Like the other coverage-job changes, this is GHA-only and validates on the PR's first CI run.

## Where the PR now stands

CI apt-install optimizations folded in: **LLVM-22 cache** (coverage job) + **dioxus-desktop-deps cache** (6 jobs). Plus everything prior: cargo-crap regression gate, `check` removal + `clippy` fan-out, native-fast coverage in `xci-fast` + `cargo xcov`, full spec sync.

Local validation status: xtask code (crap / coverage-native / cov) — 189 tests pass, clippy `-D warnings` clean, fmt OK, `coverage-native` gate green (all 8 native crates pass). CI-only bits (caches, CRAP install/gate, fan-out) validate on first CI run.

**Still open (PR-body / follow-up):** drop `Workspace Check` branch-protection check; flaky `ars-dom` wasm test (separate issue); 2.4 MB baseline.

This is a solid, coherent CI overhaul. I'd suggest we draw the line here and ship — further caching/imaging ideas (kache, custom image, coverage-split) are genuinely separate experiments.

{
  "file_path": "/tmp/ars-crap-pr-body.md",
  "content": "## Summary\n\nIntegrates the [`cargo-crap`](https://crates.io/crates/cargo-crap) CRAP-metric gate and overhauls the CI pipeline for speed and signal.\n\n### cargo-crap CRAP gate (regression mode)\n- New `xtask::crap` module: `cargo xcrap` (local report), `cargo xcrap --update-baseline`, and `cargo xtask ci crap` (the CI gate). Runs `cargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info --baseline .crap-baseline.json --fail-regression --min 30`.\n- **Regression mode, not absolute threshold.** At 100% coverage `CRAP == cyclomatic complexity`, so an absolute `--fail-above` gate would reject every well-tested `Machine::transition` (an intrinsically wide `match (state, event)`) and fight the state-machine architecture as the catalog grows. Instead the gate fails only when an already-complex (≥30) function gets *worse* than the committed baseline; new functions are tolerated (the coverage floors already ensure new code is tested). Net: coverage guarantees new code is tested; CRAP guarantees shipped code doesn't rot.\n- Pinned to `cargo-crap@0.2.2` in `xtask::crap::CARGO_CRAP_VERSION` and the workflow. `--allow 'crates/ars-derive/**'` (proc-macro, unmeasured) — `--exclude` is a no-op under `--workspace`. Gate runs in the existing `coverage` job (reuses the merged `lcov.info`, no recompile).\n- `.crap-baseline.json` committed as a native-generated safe floor (CI's merged coverage ≥ native ⇒ no false regressions); tighten from the `coverage-lcov` artifact via `cargo xcrap --update-baseline`.\n\n### CI speedup\n- **Removed the redundant `check` job** — `clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features` compiles a strict superset.\n- **Fan-out moved from `unit` to `clippy`**: release, integration, adapter, coverage, feature-flags-*, etc. now start in parallel with `unit` instead of waiting for it (clippy already guarantees compilation). Removes a full compile stage from the critical path.\n- **apt caching** for the LLVM-22 (`clang-22`/`lld-22`) install in the coverage job and the Dioxus desktop deps (GTK/WebKit) across the ~6 jobs that need them — additive, correct-by-construction (cache miss installs exactly as before).\n\n### Pre-push coverage signal (the recurring `ars-components` pain)\n- New native-only coverage check `cargo xcov` (and `cargo xcov --full` mirroring CI), now part of `cargo xci-fast`. It enforces the real thresholds of the crates whose CI coverage is native-only (the agnostic library crates + xtask), so `ars-components`-style threshold regressions surface **before** push without needing the wasm/clang-22 toolchain. Faithful to CI for exactly those crates.\n\n### Spec sync\n- `spec/testing/14-ci.md` (pipeline, fan-out, CRAP gate §2.1.1, apt caches, tools table, required-status-checks) and `spec/testing/13-policies.md` (CRAP enforcement policy) updated in lockstep. AGENTS.md documents the gate, `xcov`, and the updated fast profile.\n\n## Required manual follow-up\n- **Branch protection:** drop the `Workspace Check` required status check (the job was removed), or merges will block on a job that no longer reports.\n\n## Notes\n- Not issue-backed (user-initiated CI work), so no auto-close keyword.\n- Coverage-job changes (CRAP install/gate, apt caches, fan-out) are GHA-only and validate on this PR's first CI run; that run also produces the `coverage-lcov` artifact to tighten the baseline from.\n- Separately tracked: a flaky `ars-dom` instrumented-wasm test surfaced while diagnosing recent coverage-job failures (not addressed here to keep this PR focused).\n\n## Local verification\n- `xtask`: 189 lib tests pass, clippy `-D warnings` clean, fmt OK.\n- `cargo xtask ci coverage-native`: exit 0, all 8 native crates pass (ars-components 95.0%/90.3% vs 90/90).\n- `cargo xci-fast`: green.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\n"
}

- **(b) Separate tiny PR first**, merge it, then rebase this PR onto green main. Cleanest separation, but a merge round-trip for one line while main stays red.
- **(c) Hold** and let you handle the clippy-1.96 breakage yourself.

```

{
  "command": "cd /Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui\ngit commit \\\n -m \"ci: cargo-crap CRAP gate, drop redundant check, clippy fan-out, native coverage + apt caching\" \\\n -m \"Integrate the cargo-crap CRAP-metric gate (cyclomatic complexity vs coverage) and overhaul CI for speed and signal.\" \\\n -m \"cargo-crap: new xtask::crap module — \\`cargo xcrap\\` (report), \\`cargo xcrap --update-baseline\\`, and the \\`cargo xtask ci crap\\` CI gate running \\`--workspace --baseline .crap-baseline.json --fail-regression --min 30\\`. Regression mode (not absolute --fail-above) because at 100% coverage CRAP==cyclomatic complexity, which would reject every well-tested Machine::transition match and fight the state-machine architecture. Pinned cargo-crap@0.2.2; ars-derive suppressed via --allow (--exclude is a no-op under --workspace). Gate runs in the coverage job reusing lcov.info (no recompile). Committed native-generated baseline is a safe floor (CI merged coverage >= native => no false regressions).\" \\\n -m \"CI speedup: removed the redundant \\`check\\` job (clippy --all-targets --all-features is a strict superset); moved fan-out from the unit gate to the clippy gate so independent jobs run in parallel with unit; added apt caching for the LLVM-22 install (coverage job) and the Dioxus desktop deps (shared across ~6 jobs).\" \\\n -m \"Pre-push coverage: new native-only coverage check (\\`cargo xcov\\`, \\`cargo xcov --full\\`) added to \\`cargo xci-fast\\`, enforcing the real thresholds of the crates whose CI coverage is native-only (agnostic library crates + xtask) so ars-components-style regressions surface before push without the wasm/clang-22 toolchain.\" \\\n -m \"Spec sync: spec/testing/14-ci.md (pipeline, fan-out, CRAP gate, apt caches, required checks) and 13-policies.md (CRAP policy) updated; AGENTS.md documents the gate, xcov, and the updated fast profile.\" \\\n -m \"Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>\" 2>&1 | tail -5",

}

**<sub><sub>![P1 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P1-orange?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Regenerate the CRAP baseline with portable paths**

This baseline was generated from a different tree: it stores `/Users/.../xtask/src/main.rs` with `main` at line 535, while this commit has `fn main()` at line 550. cargo-crap's delta key includes file, function, and line (see `EntryKey` in the 0.2.2 source), and it only falls back to name-only matching for unique names, so CI running under `/home/runner/...`—and even this checkout after the line shift—will classify duplicated names such as `main` or `Machine::transition` as new/removed instead of failing on regressions. Please regenerate the committed baseline from the final tree using repo-relative/CI-normalized paths.

          Path to an LCOV coverage file (e.g. from `cargo llvm-cov --lcov --output-path lcov.info`).
      --path <DIR>
          Root directory to analyze. Defaults to the current directory
          When set, `--path` is ignored and every member crate's root is walked. Useful for monorepos: one command, one LCOV file, full workspace picture.
          Glob patterns for files to skip (relative to `--path`). Use `**` to cross directory boundaries. May be repeated.
          An entry containing `/` or `**` is treated as a path glob and matches the file in which the function is defined; otherwise it matches the function name. Path globs analyze the file but hide its functions from the report — distinct from `--exclude`, which skips files at walk time.

> AGENT

Codex is **correct** — this is a real P1: all 492 files in the committed baseline are absolute `/Users/ericson/...` paths, and cargo-crap has **no path-normalization flag**. On CI (`/home/runner/...`) the `(file, function, line)` keys would never match → every function reads as "new" → `--fail-regression` finds nothing → **the gate is a silent no-op on CI**. The baseline must use portable paths.

42	/// built-in default and the canonical `Crap4J` cutoff. Functions below this are

194	            // A complete baseline (no `--min`) is required: a function that
195	            // crosses from below the threshold to above it must be present in
196	            // the baseline at its old score, or the increase reads as "new" and
197	            // escapes the gate.
198	            args.push("--format".to_owned());
199	            args.push("json".to_owned());

207	        args.push((*glob).to_owned());
208	    }

210	    args

212	
213	/// Runs cargo-crap for `options`.
214	///
215	/// In [`Action::Gate`] mode, a missing baseline is bootstrapped: the baseline is
216	/// generated from the current lcov and the gate passes with a notice, so the
217	/// first run on a repo without a committed baseline does not fail spuriously.
218	///
219	/// # Errors
220	///
221	/// Returns [`Error::MissingTool`] when `cargo-crap` is unavailable,
222	/// [`Error::MissingLcov`] when the coverage report is absent,
223	/// [`Error::CommandFailed`] when the subprocess exits non-zero (a CRAP
224	/// regression), or [`Error::Io`] on spawn failures.
225	pub fn run(options: &Options) -> Result<(), Error> {
226	    preflight_cargo_crap()?;
227	    preflight_lcov(&options.lcov)?;
228	
229	    if options.action == Action::Gate && !options.baseline.exists() {
230	        eprintln!(
231	            "note: baseline {} not found; generating it from {} (gate is advisory until committed)",
232	            options.baseline.display(),
233	            options.lcov.display()
234	        );
235	
236	        let bootstrap = Options {
237	            action: Action::UpdateBaseline,
238	            ..options.clone()
239	        };
240	
241	        run_cargo_crap(&bootstrap)?;
242	
243	        eprintln!(
244	            "note: wrote {} — commit it to activate the regression gate",
245	            options.baseline.display()
```

  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn every_action_forwards_allow_globs() {\n        for action in [Action::Gate, Action::Report, Action::UpdateBaseline] {\n            let args = build_args(&options(action));\n            let allows = values_after(&args, \"--allow\");\n\n            for glob in ALLOW_GLOBS {\n                assert!(allows.contains(glob), \"{action:?} missing --allow {glob}\");\n            }\n\n            assert_eq!(allows.len(), ALLOW_GLOBS.len());\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn allow_globs_suppress_unmeasured_proc_macro_crate() {\n        assert!(\n            ALLOW_GLOBS.contains(&\"crates/ars-derive/**\"),\n            \"ars-derive is unmeasured by llvm-cov and must be suppressed\"\n        );\n    }",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn every_action_forwards_exclude_globs() {\n        for action in [Action::Gate, Action::Report, Action::UpdateBaseline] {\n            let args = build_args(&options(action));\n            let excludes = values_after(&args, \"--exclude\");\n\n            for glob in EXCLUDE_GLOBS {\n                assert!(\n                    excludes.contains(glob),\n                    \"{action:?} missing --exclude {glob}\"\n                );\n            }\n\n            assert_eq!(excludes.len(), EXCLUDE_GLOBS.len());\n            // `--exclude` is honored only under `--path`; never `--allow` here.\n            assert!(!has_flag(&args, \"--allow\"));\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn exclude_globs_skip_target_and_unmeasured_proc_macro_crate() {\n        assert!(\n            EXCLUDE_GLOBS.contains(&\"crates/ars-derive/**\"),\n            \"ars-derive is unmeasured by llvm-cov and must be excluded\"\n        );\n        assert!(\n            EXCLUDE_GLOBS.contains(&\"target/**\"),\n            \"build artifacts must be excluded\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn committed_baseline_uses_portable_relative_paths() {\n        // Guards Codex review #700's P1: a baseline with absolute paths\n        // (`/Users/...` or `/home/runner/...`) silently no-ops the gate on CI.\n        // Every entry's `file` must be repo-relative.\n        let baseline = std::path::Path::new(env!(\"CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR\"))\n            .join(\"..\")\n            .join(BASELINE_PATH);\n\n        let content =\n            std::fs::read_to_string(&baseline).expect(\"committed .crap-baseline.json must exist\");\n\n        let parsed: serde_json::Value =\n            serde_json::from_str(&content).expect(\"baseline must be valid JSON\");\n\n        let entries = parsed[\"entries\"]\n            .as_array()\n            .expect(\"baseline must have an entries array\");\n\n        assert!(!entries.is_empty(), \"baseline must not be empty\");\n\n        for entry in entries {\n            let file = entry[\"file\"].as_str().expect(\"entry.file must be a string\");\n            assert!(\n                !file.starts_with('/'),\n                \"baseline entry has a machine-absolute path (breaks the gate on CI): {file}\"\n            );\n        }\n    }"
}

AGENTS.md:106:- **Policy:** `cargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info --baseline .crap-baseline.json --fail-regression --min 30`. The gate fails only when a function **at or above CRAP 30 regresses** (more complexity or less coverage) versus the committed baseline. New functions are tolerated — the per-crate coverage thresholds already ensure new code is tested — and sub-threshold churn on simple functions is ignored. Net: the coverage gate guarantees new code is tested; the CRAP gate guarantees shipped code does not rot.

  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui/AGENTS.md",
  "old_string": "- **Policy:** `cargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info --baseline .crap-baseline.json --fail-regression --min 30`. The gate fails only when a function **at or above CRAP 30 regresses** (more complexity or less coverage) versus the committed baseline. New functions are tolerated — the per-crate coverage thresholds already ensure new code is tested — and sub-threshold churn on simple functions is ignored. Net: the coverage gate guarantees new code is tested; the CRAP gate guarantees shipped code does not rot.",
  "new_string": "- **Policy:** `cargo crap --path . --lcov lcov.info --baseline .crap-baseline.json --fail-regression --min 30 --exclude 'target/**' --exclude 'crates/ars-derive/**'`. The gate fails only when a function **at or above CRAP 30 regresses** (more complexity or less coverage) versus the committed baseline. New functions are tolerated — the per-crate coverage thresholds already ensure new code is tested — and sub-threshold churn on simple functions is ignored. Net: the coverage gate guarantees new code is tested; the CRAP gate guarantees shipped code does not rot.\n- **`--path .`, not `--workspace`.** cargo-crap's regression key is `(file, function, line)`, and `--workspace` records machine-absolute file paths (`/Users/…` locally, `/home/runner/…` on CI). A baseline committed with absolute paths never matches on CI, silently turning the gate into a no-op. `--path .` emits repo-relative paths (`./crates/…`) that match on any checkout; coverage still associates correctly because cargo-crap canonicalizes paths when reading the lcov. `--exclude` is honored under `--path` (it is a no-op under `--workspace`), so `target/**` and the unmeasured `crates/ars-derive/**` are skipped there. Because the key includes the line number, a baseline only matches non-uniquely-named functions (e.g. `Machine::transition`) while their line numbers are stable; regenerate the baseline when a tracked file's lines shift materially."

```
494	coverage step just produced, so it never recompiles — it only parses source and
495	the lcov tracefile. The CRAP score is `complexity² · (1 − coverage)³ +
496	complexity`.
497	
498	The gate runs in **regression mode**, not absolute-threshold mode:
499	
500	```sh
501	cargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info \
502	    --baseline .crap-baseline.json --fail-regression --min 30 \
503	    --allow 'crates/ars-derive/**'
504	```
505	
506	It fails the job only when a function **at or above CRAP 30 regresses** (more
507	complexity or less coverage) versus the committed `.crap-baseline.json`. New
508	functions are tolerated — the per-crate coverage thresholds (§2.2) already
509	ensure new code is tested — and sub-threshold churn on simple functions is
510	ignored. The rationale: at 100% coverage `CRAP == cyclomatic complexity`, so an
511	absolute `--fail-above` threshold would reject every well-tested `Machine::transition`
512	(an intrinsically wide `match (state, event)`), fighting the state-machine
513	architecture and worsening as the component catalog grows. Regression mode
514	instead guarantees shipped code does not rot while the coverage gate guarantees
515	new code is tested.
```

  "old_string": "```sh\ncargo crap --workspace --lcov lcov.info \\\n    --baseline .crap-baseline.json --fail-regression --min 30 \\\n    --allow 'crates/ars-derive/**'\n```\n\nIt fails the job only when a function **at or above CRAP 30 regresses** (more\ncomplexity or less coverage) versus the committed `.crap-baseline.json`. New\nfunctions are tolerated — the per-crate coverage thresholds (§2.2) already\nensure new code is tested — and sub-threshold churn on simple functions is\nignored. The rationale: at 100% coverage `CRAP == cyclomatic complexity`, so an\nabsolute `--fail-above` threshold would reject every well-tested `Machine::transition`\n(an intrinsically wide `match (state, event)`), fighting the state-machine\narchitecture and worsening as the component catalog grows. Regression mode\ninstead guarantees shipped code does not rot while the coverage gate guarantees\nnew code is tested.",
  "new_string": "```sh\ncargo crap --path . --lcov lcov.info \\\n    --baseline .crap-baseline.json --fail-regression --min 30 \\\n    --exclude 'target/**' --exclude 'crates/ars-derive/**'\n```\n\n`--path .` (not `--workspace`) is load-bearing: cargo-crap's regression key is\n`(file, function, line)`, and `--workspace` records machine-absolute paths\n(`/Users/…` locally, `/home/runner/…` on CI), so a committed baseline never\nmatches on CI and the gate silently no-ops. `--path .` emits repo-relative paths\n(`./crates/…`) that match on any checkout; coverage still associates because\ncargo-crap canonicalizes paths when reading the lcov. `--exclude` is honored\nunder `--path` (a no-op under `--workspace`), so `target/**` and the unmeasured\n`crates/ars-derive/**` are skipped there.\n\nIt fails the job only when a function **at or above CRAP 30 regresses** (more\ncomplexity or less coverage) versus the committed `.crap-baseline.json`. New\nfunctions are tolerated — the per-crate coverage thresholds (§2.2) already\nensure new code is tested — and sub-threshold churn on simple functions is\nignored. The rationale: at 100% coverage `CRAP == cyclomatic complexity`, so an\nabsolute `--fail-above` threshold would reject every well-tested `Machine::transition`\n(an intrinsically wide `match (state, event)`), fighting the state-machine\narchitecture and worsening as the component catalog grows. Regression mode\ninstead guarantees shipped code does not rot while the coverage gate guarantees\nnew code is tested. Because the key includes the line number, the committed\nbaseline should be regenerated (`cargo xcrap --update-baseline`) when a tracked\nfile's lines shift materially, so non-uniquely-named functions keep matching."
}
```

> AGENT

Confirmed the fix works at the arg level (all logic tests pass; the baseline-content test correctly fails on the still-absolute committed baseline). I'm regenerating a full native lcov now (background) to rebuild the baseline with portable paths — not running anything else concurrently to avoid the build-lock stall from before.

**Status on Codex's P1 (PR #700):** Confirmed real and serious — our committed baseline had machine-absolute `/Users/…` paths and cargo-crap keys on `(file, function, line)`, so on CI (`/home/runner/…`) nothing would match and `--fail-regression` would silently catch nothing (gate = no-op). Plus the baseline was generated from a pre-edit tree, so even line numbers were stale.

**Fix in flight:**
- Switched the gate/baseline/report from `--workspace` (absolute paths) to `--path .` (repo-relative `./crates/…`, portable across local + CI). Verified coverage still associates and `target/` doesn't leak.
- Moved suppression from `--allow` to real `--exclude` (honored under `--path`): `target/**` + `crates/ars-derive/**`.
- Added a guard test that reads the committed baseline and fails if any path is absolute.
- Synced AGENTS.md + 14-ci.md.
- Regenerating `.crap-baseline.json` from the final tree with relative paths now.

Once the lcov finishes I'll rebuild the baseline, confirm the gate is green + all xtask tests pass, commit, push, reply on the Codex thread, and re-trigger `@codex review`. I'll also note the one residual cargo-crap limitation (line-number-keyed matching) that the docs now cover. Standing by for the lcov build.
